/** * RestWerks Portal API * Version: 1.71.0 (2026-09-05_1930 CT) * * 1.71.0 - NUDGES BECOME A SETTING. Schema and routes only: NOTHING IN THIS BUILD SENDS * ANYTHING. The sweep that reads these rows is off in webhook v9.30.2 and stays off * until the duplicate-send claim is diagnosed. Pairs with dashboard v1.77.0. * * WHY THIS EXISTS: v9.30.0 shipped a nudge that texted Todd Moitz, a real client, while * his Ticket 25 had a confirmed visit booked for Sep 10. He replied "Close it? No one has * come yet." He was not ignoring us, he was WAITING ON US, and we asked him whether it * had sorted itself out. It also sent twice, and the copy offered to close a ticket, * which Andi is never allowed to do. * * THE DESIGN INVERTED, and that is the whole change rather than a tuned interval. The old * trigger was SILENCE: Andi asked, nobody answered, chase them. That is a blocklist, and * a blocklist means nudging by default and subtracting the wrong cases one real client at * a time. Andy's call is an allowlist: a client is nudged only where a HUMAN has put the * ticket in a status that says the client owes us something. Every state nobody thought * about is silent. The failure mode flips from texting somebody who is waiting on us to * occasionally not texting somebody who is stalling, and only one of those gets you * called a dummy by a real client. * * SO THE STATUS IS THE TRIGGER, not the conversation. Two independent flags per status * row, nudge_client and nudge_team, and a per-status interval in hours. Andy: full * control to the integrator of as much as possible. Keying on the literal key * 'waiting_client' was the alternative and it is the same mistake as reading status by * key instead of kind, which cost us a version on Sep 5: statuses are editable rows, a * shop can rename Waiting on Client or invent their own, and a flag survives that. * * THE FLAGS ARE REFUSED WHERE THEY WOULD BE A LIE. Not on the two endings, because a * finished ticket is not waiting on anybody. Not on Scheduled for the CLIENT, which is * exactly the Todd case written as a server-enforced rule rather than a comment: a * client with a booked visit is waiting on us and cannot be nudged even if somebody * ticks the box. The TEAM flag is allowed on Scheduled, since a booked visit the shop has * not prepared for is a real thing to be reminded about. * * THE CLOCK STARTS AT status_changed_at, not at the last message. The nudge measures how * long the ticket has been in THAT status, which is the thing the status is claiming. * It also means nothing that happened before this deploy is ever eligible, which kills * the other half of the Todd incident: the old sweep had no floor on staleness, so on its * first tick every historically silent thread became eligible at once. * * CLIENT INTERVAL IS NOT SCALED BY URGENCY, by Andy. Priority is the shop's own internal * severity that the client never sees or sets, so chasing a client harder because WE * called it critical is us making our urgency their problem. Urgency scales the TEAM * nudge only, which is where it means something. Seeded at 24h client, and the team * multipliers stay in the webhook where the sweep is. * * MASTER SWITCH, integrators.nudges_enabled, DEFAULT FALSE FOR EVERYONE INCLUDING * EXISTING ROWS. Off beats on given how this feature introduced itself. Nobody's first * week should contain an automated text they did not ask for, and after tonight nobody's * existing week should either. * * STAGES ONE AND TWO ONLY. The third stage, a closing notice, is deliberately NOT here: * it needs auto-close, auto-close needs reopen, and reopen is its own build touching * ticket_status_events and the resolution clock. A text that announces a close nothing * performs is an empty promise, which is the same defect as the copy that went to Todd. * * 1.70.1 - THE MEDIA NORMALIZER THREW THE MEDIA AWAY. * Found live within minutes of webhook v9.30.1: a captioned photo still did not render, * and a hard refresh showed it, which proved the R2 object, the refs and the row were all * correct and put the fault squarely on this route. * * THE FAULT WAS MINE AND IT IS WORTH NAMING PRECISELY. v1.70.0 accepted the media array * and mapped each entry through (m.url || m.ref || m.key) to pull out a string. The * webhook's uploadMmsToR2 returns { r2_key, mime_type }. None of those three fields * exist on it, so every entry mapped to null, the filter dropped every null, and a * non-empty array arrived here and left as an empty one. The dashboard then correctly * saw no media and did nothing. I invented a ref shape instead of reading the one the * webhook actually produces, and because the failure was silent it looked exactly like * the webhook not sending the field at all. * * THE FIX IS NOT TO ADD r2_key TO THE LIST. That would fix this instance and leave the * class: this route does not own the media shape and has no business parsing it. The * only thing any consumer needs from this payload is WHETHER there is media, because the * refs are R2 keys and the browser-usable signed URL is minted by the thread read, not * here. So the array is now passed through as-is after a shape-agnostic sanity check * (it is an array, it is capped, entries are strings or plain objects) and no field is * reached into. A webhook that changes its ref shape tomorrow cannot break this again. * * 1.70.0 - THE TAXONOMY GETS A TABLE, AND THE PHOTO NOTIFY CARRIES ITS MEDIA. * Ship 3, portal half. Two unrelated things ride together because they are both * portal-only and both have to land before the dashboard and webhook halves can. * * (1) THE CATEGORY TAXONOMY IS DATA NOW. New ticket_categories and * ticket_subcategories, one row per integrator, seeded with the eleven parents plus * Other and about eighty children. Same shape as ticket_statuses and for the same * reason: a category the shop can see is a row, not a literal in fourteen places. * THE PARENTS ARE LOCKED AND THERE IS NO ROUTE THAT ADDS OR DELETES ONE. Every * analytic is a ratio across time or across shops, and a parent that splits in June * makes every prior comparison dishonest. What a shop CAN do is hide a parent it does * not service (AVDCI may not do access control, someone will do pool and spa) and * split a child finer. Neither moves a historical row. A subcategory only ever * SPLITS, never re-parents: carving Video over IP out of Video is safe because old * rows stay under Video and parent totals never move. * * THE DUPLICATION IS DELIBERATE. Camera, Doorbell, NVR, Remote viewing, Gate control * and Door station appear under BOTH Security and Surveillance, because the parent * carries the PLATFORM and the platform decides which tech goes. Andy: if the camera * or doorbell is on alarm.com we send our security technician, if it is not we send * the AO technician. Dispatch falls straight out of the category with no second field * to keep in sync. The cost is that a brand reliability query on cameras sums across * two parents, which is a query problem and not a taxonomy problem. * * MULTIPLE SUBCATEGORIES, ONE PRIMARY. A distributed audio fault genuinely lives in * amplifier and distribution at once. The PRIMARY lives on the ticket as a plain * column (tickets.category, tickets.subcategory) so every chart is a column read and * never a join, and the extras ride in ticket_subcategory_links for pattern * recognition and brand data. The number that gets divided has to be unambiguous; the * knowledge that makes Andi smart does not. * * NOTHING IS BACKFILLED, ON PURPOSE. tickets.category is new and stays NULL on every * existing row. The old flat vocabulary (battery_alert, reboot_required and the rest) * was a SYMPTOM list, not a category list, and reboot_required is an OUTCOME rather * than a place in the house. Judgment cannot be backfilled onto old rows, so guessing * a parent for four years of history would manufacture a number nobody could defend. * Old rows read as uncategorised, which is true. * * scoreCategory AND CATEGORY_TERMS STAY IN THIS FILE FOR NOW. They are the temporary * second copy of the webhook's detectCategory and they die when the webhook starts * storing the category on write. They cannot die in THIS deploy: the portal ships * before the webhook, so deleting them now would blank the home panel category * section for the window between the two deploys. * * (2) THE NOTIFY CARRIES THE MEDIA REFS. Half of Lance's August photo report, never * fixed. The R2 upload, the refs and the row were all correct, confirmed by hard * refresh; the SSE payload simply carried no media, and the dashboard's 1.2 second * soft reload only fired on the bare [photo] message shape, so a photo WITH a caption * never triggered it and sat invisible until the next message forced a reload. * /api/notify now accepts an optional media array and puts it on the new_message * event, so the dashboard can render from the event instead of inferring from the * text. Optional and additive: a notify with no media behaves exactly as before, and * the enrichment can never fail its host route. * * 1.69.0 - OPEN AND SCHEDULED KEEP THEIR COLOURS (pairs with dashboard v1.70.0). * * Andy, on being asked whether a recoloured status should follow through to the sidebar * badges: he does not want Open and Scheduled recolourable at all, for support reasons. * Right, and it is the better answer than syncing the badges would have been. Those two * colours are a language the whole dashboard already speaks (the conversation dots, the * section headers, the nav badges, the ticket pills), and a shop that turns Open green * makes every screenshot they ever send unreadable to the person helping them. The * colour editing only ever existed for CUSTOM statuses, where the server picks a default * and the shop would otherwise be stuck with it; it got applied to all six by reflex. * * THE GUARD IS HERE, NOT ONLY IN THE UI. The dashboard stops showing the picker on those * two rows in v1.70.0, but a rule enforced only in the dashboard is a rule until someone * hits the API. kind 'open' and kind 'scheduled' now reject a colour change outright. * Their LABELS stay editable, which is the split that matters: what a shop calls Open is * theirs, what colour Open is belongs to everyone who has to read their screen. * * Resolved and Closed stay recolourable. They are locked against removal and against * changing meaning, but nothing outside the ticket surfaces reads their colour, so there * is no support argument for freezing them. * * Priority colours are untouched here and simply stop being offered in the dashboard: * Low through Critical is a scale, the four seeded colours already read as one, and * nothing keyed to them exists yet. * * 1.68.0 - THE STATUS MODEL STOPS BEING DERIVED (portal only; dashboard half follows and does * NOT deploy before this). * * THE RULE THAT GOES AWAY. Since 1.15.0 a ticket has been Scheduled if and only if it * carried a scheduled_at. That was a good rule while there were two states. It cannot * survive Waiting on Parts, which is not a date, so status becomes a real field a human * sets and scheduled_at becomes a PROPERTY of the ticket rather than the definition of * its state. The convenience is kept: setting a date still flips a live ticket to * Scheduled on its own, and clearing the date on a Scheduled ticket returns it to Open. * Clearing a date on a ticket parked in Waiting on Parts just clears the date. * * STATUSES ARE DATA, NOT A CONSTRAINT. New ticket_statuses, one row per status per * integrator: key, label, kind, color, sort order, counts_toward_clock, locked. AO seeds * with six. FOUR ARE LOCKED because the product reasons about them and cannot be taught * a new meaning by a settings screen: open, scheduled, resolved, closed. Everything * between the beginning and the two endings is the shop's own. A status an integrator * adds is always kind 'waiting', which is the only kind that can be created, and that * single restriction is what keeps every count, badge and rate downstream honest. * tickets_status_check is DROPPED: a CHECK constraint on literal words is exactly the * thing that made 'scheduled' 500 in 1.14.3, and it cannot be maintained against a table * the customer edits. Validation moves into the route, where the error can be worded. * * EVERY COUNT NOW READS KIND, NEVER THE WORD. /api/stats, the household badges and * /api/home-stats all resolved status by string comparison, so a shop adding one status * would have silently dropped those tickets out of every total. They join ticket_statuses * and count kinds, with a CASE fallback so a row whose status has no matching table entry * still lands somewhere sane instead of vanishing. Absence of a seed is never a zero. * * CLOSED IS A REAL ENDING, SEPARATE FROM RESOLVED. A ticket that ends without a fix is * ordinary: a duplicate, a client who handled it themselves, a cancelled job, a wrong * number. Today the only exits are Resolved or leave it open forever, so those all get * filed as Resolved, which inflates the resolution rate AND, worse, forces somebody to * type a fix note that never existed. That sentence is the artifact the learning pool * eats. Closed requires a REASON and not a fix, writes no resolution record, does not * count as resolved in the rate, and is excluded from resolution time rather than * counted as zero. It keeps garbage out of Andi's brain, which is the real point. * * THE CLOCK IS A SUM OVER INTERVALS. ticket_status_events shipped empty in 1.64.0 and is * now written on every transition, each row carrying whether that status counted. The * running total accumulates INTO tickets.resolution_seconds at the moment of each * change (clock_running_since closes out, the new status either restarts it or leaves it * null), so a read is one column plus the live tail and never a replay of the events. * The events remain the audit trail and are the ticket history John has never had. * BACKFILL: every existing ticket gets a synthetic opening event, terminal ones get a * closing event at closed_at and the naive closed-minus-opened number as their history, * which is honest because nothing better exists for a ticket nobody was clocking. * * URGENCY IS NOT A STATUS. New ticket_priorities, same shape, four seeded, and a * priority column on the ticket. A ticket can be Waiting on Parts AND critical; those * are independent axes and collapsing them is what made 'escalated' useless. ESCALATED * IS RETIRED: it sat in the constraint and twelve places in this file, appeared once in * the dashboard, and never once in the webhook. Existing rows migrate to open. The real * need behind it, a ticket that has to jump the queue, is what priority is for. * * NOT IN THIS BUILD, ON PURPOSE: the webhook. Andi does not learn the new statuses, * does not triage urgency, and the nudge crons are untouched. That is one webhook pass * and the ordering rule is that the four Aug 20 live tests run before anything touches * it. That rule exists because the Aug 20 claim guard introduced the exact bug it was * written to prevent. * * 1.67.0 - BEST MATCH WINS, NOT FIRST MATCH (portal only, no dashboard change). * * THE BUG, found by printing every summary next to the category it lands in rather than * guessing from the chart: * "loft shade that won't go down and a broken great room remote" -> control_system * "primary bedroom remote was completely dead" -> audio * The first is a SHADE issue that lost to the word remote, because control sits above shade * in the ladder. The second is a CONTROL issue that lost to audio, which sits higher still. * Neither is a missing keyword. The ladder returns whichever rule sits HIGHEST, not * whichever fits BEST, so the category a summary lands in depends on the order I happened * to write fourteen lines in. Adding words could never fix that; it would move the * collisions around. * * THE FIX: score every category by how many of its terms appear, and take the highest * score. Ties fall back to ladder order, which keeps the old behavior as the tiebreaker * rather than the whole rule. "loft shade that won't go down and a broken great room * remote" scores shade 1 and control 1, so the tie sends it to control by order, which is * why shade also gains the phrase "won't go down" and "wont go down" as a second signal. * THE PRINCIPLE: the DEVICE that is broken beats a device merely mentioned, and the more * specific match beats the more general one. * * TWO OTHERS ARE CORRECT AND STAY OTHER, which matters as much as the fixes. "Client asked * a trivia question about Norway's capital" and "client texted the wrong number after * getting a new phone" are not home system issues, and forcing them into a bucket to make a * chart look tidy is the same instinct as the 83 percent that started this. Other is an * honest answer when the thing genuinely is other. * * NOT FIXED HERE, and it is a different problem: "The client had an issue that was tracked * as Ticket 27" is Andi summarizing without naming the issue. No categorizer can rescue * that. It belongs with the summary quality work on the Sept 8 learning pass. * * 1.66.0 - CATEGORIZE FROM ANDI'S OWN SUMMARY, NOT FROM RAW CLIENT TEXT (portal only; the dashboard * needs no change, the payload shape is identical). * * WHY THE PREVIOUS TWO ATTEMPTS BOTH FAILED, because the answer was not a longer keyword * list. 1.63.0 read the first client message and put six of nine sessions in Other. 1.64.0 * read every client message in the session and still left four in Other. Pulling the actual * sessions is what settled it, and they looked like this: * "It doesn't | Not available until 930 | Yes | Thank you" * "What's the capitol of Norway? | No. Thank you | Why did you answer me" * "That works | This system sucks ass | What's my WiFi" * Those are not issues. They are the tail ends of conversations, and unrelated ones mashed * together. THE TELL WAS THE TIMESTAMP: nearly every summary is stamped 07:00, because it * is written by the MORNING CRON rather than at the end of a conversation. So a "session" * bounded by summary rows is not a conversation at all, it is a time slice defined by when * a cron job runs, and its 24 hour lookback swept up whatever talk fell inside it. No * category list can fix that, because the input is not one issue. It is a day. * * THE RIGHT INPUT WAS ALREADY THERE. analyzeConversation writes a structured summary and * one of its fields is `issue`: Andi's own one line statement of what the conversation was * about, written by the model that handled it, with the small talk and the stress testing * already stripped out. The Resolved by Andi page has rendered that field for months. It is * strictly better input than raw client text and it needs no new data. * So the ladder now runs over `message::json->>'issue'` on the summary row. Rows whose JSON * will not parse, or that carry no issue, are counted as Uncategorized rather than dropped, * which keeps the categories summing to the hero count. * * WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, and it should be said plainly rather than discovered later: the * 07:00 cron summary still bundles a whole day into ONE summary, so a day holding two * genuinely different issues produces one issue line and counts once. Real session * boundaries are a webhook concern, not a portal one, and they belong with the learning * layer work on Sept 8. This build makes the category honest about what the summary says; * it does not make the summary finer grained than it is. * * ALSO WORTH KNOWING WHEN READING THE CHART TODAY: most AO traffic on this number is Andy * testing. The mechanism is correct now; the input stays thin until real clients are on the * line. * * 1.65.0 - ANDI STATS, AND AFTER HOURS SPLIT INTO ITS REAL PARTS (portal only; pairs with dashboard * v1.65.0, DEPLOY THIS FIRST). * * A CORRECTION TO 1.64.0, and the mistake is worth writing down. Andy said Andi's average * response time was not impressive and that he did not care how many messages a fix took. * Both true. I removed those two rows and then removed the CARD they lived in, which took * households served and total households with it, and scattered the rest of Andi's numbers * into the hero and the ticket card. The Andi section ended up as a single chart, when it * should be the richest part of the page. Two bad rows is not a bad card. * * AFTER HOURS IS NOW THREE NUMBERS INSTEAD OF ONE. 1.64.0 lumped every evening, weekend and * middle-of-the-night conversation into one bucket, which buries the most striking one. A * Saturday afternoon and a 2am Tuesday are both outside business hours and they are not the * same story: one is a client who did not have to wait until Monday, the other is a client * who did not have to wait at all, and nobody at the office woke up for either. * - after_hours: everything outside 7am to 5pm weekdays. The headline share. * - overnight: 9pm to 6am, the ones that would have been a morning callback. * - weekend: Saturday and Sunday. * All evaluated in the INTEGRATOR's timezone, not the server's, because 2am matters only * relative to where the client actually sleeps. * Overnight and weekend overlap on purpose (a 2am Sunday is both) and they are reported as * two separate views of the same set rather than forced into exclusive buckets, since the * question each answers is different and forcing exclusivity would make both misleading. * * 1.64.0 - THE TICKET CLOCK, AFTER HOURS, AND ISSUES THAT READ THE WHOLE SESSION (portal only; * pairs with dashboard v1.64.0, DEPLOY THIS FIRST). * * (1) THE TICKET CLOCK, STRUCTURE NOW AND THE NUMBER LATER. Andy's rule: do not push work * off because the data lands later, build the column and wire it in. So the schema and the * response half ship tonight and the resolution half fills in when the statuses land. * RESPONSE TIME IS REAL TONIGHT: ticket opened to the first human reply on that thread. * It needs nothing that does not already exist, so it is computed AND backfilled onto every * existing ticket on boot (idempotent, only where null, so it runs once and then no-ops). * RESOLUTION TIME IS PROVISIONED AND NULL. Deliberately, because the number I could compute * today is closed_at minus opened_at, and that is precisely the number John is complaining * about in Freshdesk: a ticket waiting three weeks on a backordered part reads as a three * week resolution and makes AO look slow at something that was never theirs. His ask is * accumulated time with the clock STOPPED on Waiting on Client, Scheduled With Client and * Waiting on Parts. Freshdesk carries a stop-the-clock field of its own, which is how we * know that is what he means. * New `ticket_status_events`, one row per status change with entered_at and a * counts_toward_clock flag, which makes the clock a SUM over intervals instead of a single * subtraction, and makes it auditable after the fact rather than a number nobody can check. * New columns on tickets: first_response_at, first_response_seconds, resolution_seconds * (null until the statuses ship), clock_running_since. * THE STOP-THE-CLOCK DEFAULTS ARE EVIDENCE, NOT A GUESS: those three status names are in * AO's own Freshdesk export, so they are what the office already says out loud. * KNOWN COLLISION, recorded rather than worked around: ticket status is DERIVED from * scheduled_at (since portal 1.15.0) and these statuses cannot be derived. Unwinding the * derived model is the Saturday build. This schema is laid so that work is a wiring job. * * (2) AFTER HOURS SHARE. Replaces two rows nobody reads. Andi's average response time is * 3.3 seconds, which is not a metric, it is a description of a computer, and messages to * resolve is a number Andy said plainly he does not care about: if it got fixed, the count * does not matter. Both are removed from the payload, not just hidden, so nothing computes * a number no one reads. What goes in their place is the one number that IS the product: * how many of Andi's conversations happened while the office was closed. Evaluated against * the integrator's own timezone and a 7am to 5pm weekday business day. * Response time comes BACK, as a before and after pair, once the Freshdesk archive gives it * something to sit against: 3.3 seconds versus AO's real historical answer time on the same * clients. Alone it is noise, paired it is the whole client care story. * * (3) ISSUES READ THE WHOLE SESSION. 1.63.0 categorized from the FIRST client message, * which put six of nine AO sessions in Other, because an opening line is usually "you * there" or "having an issue with the theater" and the real subject arrives in message two * or three. Categorization now runs over every client message in the session concatenated. * Uncategorized sessions now surface as their own row instead of being dropped, which fixes * the quiet mismatch where the hero said 11 conversations and the categories summed to 9. * * 1.63.0 - VOLUME, AND ISSUES BY CATEGORY (portal only; pairs with dashboard v1.63.0, DEPLOY THIS * FIRST). Feeds the reshaped home panel: an ANDI section for what he handled and a TICKET * section for what escalated. * * (1) VOLUME. The panel could show a resolution rate and no idea how many conversations it * was a rate OF. A percentage with no volume under it is the thing a skeptical owner is * right to distrust, and it is also the number that climbs, which is the reason to open the * page tomorrow. Two counts added: conversations_handled (sessions Andi closed, the same * unit as the resolution rate so the two read together) and messages_handled (every client * and Andi turn in the window, the bigger number, and the one that means a text somebody at * the office did not answer at 10pm). * * (2) ISSUES BY CATEGORY, DERIVED ON READ, and this needs its caveat stated plainly. * The webhook HAS a category detector and throws its answer away: saveResolution is called * with null, which is why the four categories this product is built around have never been * retrievable. The one line fix is scheduled for the Sept 8 webhook touch, and every webhook * change is gated behind live tests, so waiting means an empty card for a week. * So the portal derives it instead, from the FIRST client message of each closed session, * using a SQL ladder that mirrors the webhook's detectCategory in the same order with the * same patterns. Same input, same rules, same answer. * THE HONEST COST: this is a SECOND copy of that pattern list and it can drift from the * webhook's copy. It is deliberately temporary. When the webhook stores the category on * write, this ladder is deleted and the stored column becomes the single source. Until then * the duplication is the price of a real card a week early, and it is written down here so * nobody finds it in six months and wonders which copy is authoritative. Neither is: the * stored column will be. * Categorized per SESSION, not per message, so one client saying battery four times in one * conversation is one battery issue and not four. * * Both counts and the category list return empty rather than a placeholder when nothing has * happened yet, because zeros are the honest start for a new integrator and a zero that can * climb is the point of the panel. * * 1.62.0 - THE TWO DEFINITIONS 1.61.0 GOT WRONG (portal only, NO dashboard change: the JSON keys are * identical, so dashboard v1.62.0 renders the corrected numbers with no edit). * * Both were caught by Andy on the live panel within minutes of deploy, and both are the same * mistake as the fiction this build set out to remove: a number that computes cleanly and * means nothing. Worth naming, because a wrong denominator is harder to spot than a * hardcoded 83 percent. The fake number announces itself. This one does not. * * (1) RESOLUTION RATE READ 100 PERCENT ON EVERY ROW. It was summary rows over distinct * THREADS, which on AO was 11 over 4, or 275 percent, flattened to 100 by the cap. The cap * hid the bug rather than revealing it. A thread is a phone number and lives for months * across many separate sessions, so it was never a denominator, it was sessions per thread. * THE CORRECT DEFINITION IS THE ONE THE CARD ALREADY CLAIMS, "Andi resolved without a * tech": of the sessions Andi CLOSED, the share that did not produce a ticket. Numerator and * denominator are now the same unit, a session, which is what makes it a rate at all. A * session counts as escalated when a ticket was opened on that phone between the session * start and ten minutes past its close, the grace covering a ticket written as the session * ends. This number can now fall, which the old one structurally could not. * * (2) AVG MESSAGES TO RESOLVE READ 30.6. The window ran from the PREVIOUS summary on that * thread, and the first session on any thread has no previous summary, so it counted every * message that phone number had ever sent, back to June. Andy's own live figure earlier * tonight was 3.9 seconds average response and roughly a four message exchange, so 30.6 was * visibly wrong to anyone who knows the threads, which is exactly why he caught it and I did * not. A first session now looks back 24 hours instead of forever. A session is a * conversation; nobody spends three months resolving one shade. * * THE STANDING LESSON, and it is the reason this entry is long: this build replaced numbers * that were invented with numbers that were computed, and assumed computed meant true. * Computed and wrong is the more dangerous of the two, because it survives review. Every * rate needs its denominator named out loud in the code, which is now done for both. * * 1.61.0 - THE HOME PANEL GETS REAL NUMBERS (portal only; pairs with dashboard v1.62.0, DEPLOY THIS * FIRST). One new route, GET /api/home-stats. No schema change, no existing route touched. * * WHY: every number on the dashboard home panel is hardcoded HTML and always has been. 83 * percent resolution rate, 14 truck rolls avoided this month, 4.2 messages to resolve, 100 * percent after hours coverage, the whole issue category chart. None of it is computed and * none of it moves. It is the same failure class as the 2pm incident, where Andi stated a * resolved June ticket as a live 2pm appointment: fluent, specific, and not true. The * difference is that this one is the FIRST screen an integrator sees, and on Sept 9 John * and Chris start landing on it from every push notification. * * WHAT IS COMPUTED, and from what: * - conversations: distinct phone numbers with a client message in the window. * - resolved by Andi: summary rows written in the window. A summary is what Andi writes * when he closes a session, so it is the honest definition of one he handled. * - resolution rate: resolved sessions over threads that had client traffic, capped at 100 * and returned as null (not zero) when there is no traffic to divide by. Also computed * for 7 days, 30 days and all time, so the trend rows stop being three invented numbers * that always rise. * - avg response time: seconds from a client message to Andi's next message, pairs over * five minutes excluded as not-a-reply. Measured live tonight at 3.9 seconds average. * - avg messages per resolved session: real messages between one summary and the next. * - top ticket categories: tickets.issue_category for THIS integrator. Note the honest * label change on the dashboard side: these are TICKET categories, meaning things Andi * escalated, not a survey of everything clients ask about. The old card implied the * latter while showing neither. * - households, active households, open tickets, follow ups: straight counts. * * WHAT IS DELETED RATHER THAN COMPUTED, and this is the important half: * - TRUCK ROLLS AVOIDED. There is no truck roll model. Counting resolved sessions as * avoided truck rolls would inflate the number by every battery question a client would * never have called about. The real version is the time saved model in workstream 2, * which needs the calibration pass against real threads. Until that exists this number * cannot be honest, so it does not appear. This is the number Andy will most want back; * it comes back when it is earned. * - AFTER HOURS COVERAGE 100 PERCENT. Andi answers whenever he is not muted, so the number * is either meaningless or a tautology. Gone. * * EMPTY IS EMPTY. Every field returns null rather than a zero or a placeholder when there * is nothing to compute, so a brand new integrator sees blanks instead of a fabricated * first impression. That rule is the whole point of this build. * * SCALE: one route, six queries, all filtered by integrator_id and all bounded by a time * window. The window is validated against a whitelist so the interval can never be built * from user input. * * 1.60.0 - EMAIL ON THE PERSON, THE IMPORT SCHEMA, AND ARCHIVE STORAGE (portal only, NO dashboard change, * NO webhook change). The Sept 2 slice of the Sept 9 AO cutover sprint. Purely additive: three new * concerns land as new tables and new routes, and every existing route behaves exactly as it did. * * (1) EMAIL ON THE PERSON RECORD. Until now email existed only as a loose contact_email string on * tickets and follow_ups, so there was no way to say WHO an address belongs to. The Freshdesk export * is the forcing function: 801 of 817 contacts carry an email and only 186 carry a phone, so email * has to be a first class key on the person or the archive cannot attach to anybody. * client_emails MIRRORS client_phones on purpose: same shape, same orphan story. A person can hold * more than one address, one is primary, and a row can exist with client_id NULL as an email first * orphan the same way client_phones already holds a phone first orphan from a client texting in. * email_norm (lowercased, trimmed) is the stored match key and is what every lookup index is built * on, so matching never depends on how a human typed it. * NO DATABASE UNIQUE CONSTRAINT, deliberately, and this is the one place the email model departs * from the phone model. A phone number reaches one handset, so unique per integrator is true. An * email address is routinely shared (a couple on one address, an office alias), and a hard * constraint would make a 949 ticket import fail on a legitimate row at 3am. The manual UI path * still gets the same friendly 409 the phone path gives, enforced in code where it can be worded; * the import path is free to record reality and route the ambiguity to a review pile instead. * Reads: GET /api/households/:id and GET /api/household-by-phone now return `email` (the primary * active address, or null) and `emails` (the full list) on every person. Writes: add person and * edit person take `email`; deactivate and reactivate carry emails the way they carry phones. * NOT touched this slice: the attach and create household paths (their UIs have no email field * yet) and the dashboard, which is a separate deploy. * * (2) IMPORT SCHEMA. Four tables that hold an external export and the DECISION made about each row, * which is the part that has to survive: import_batches (one uploaded file), import_contacts * (staging, cascades with its batch), archive_tickets and archive_notes (the archive itself). * John's rule is that Freshdesk history arrives as DATA, not as working tickets, so none of this * writes into `tickets`; the archive is its own read only body of record and a working ticket is * only ever created by a human or by Andi, as today. * TWO RULES ARE IN THE SCHEMA RATHER THAN IN THE IMPORTER, so no future importer can quietly break * them. First, nothing here can write opt in status or a textable flag: there is no such column on * any of these tables and the import path never touches client_phones. Eight hundred unvalidated * numbers arriving is a temptation, and none of those people consented; consent stays inbound first. * Second, every row carries review_status defaulting to 'pending', so an unmatched or ambiguous row * PARKS instead of guessing a household. * raw JSONB on every staging row keeps the source record verbatim, so a re derivation later never * needs the CSV again. (integrator_id, source, source_ticket_id) is UNIQUE on archive_tickets, so a * re run updates in place instead of doubling the archive. * * (3) ARCHIVE STORAGE. POST /api/imports/upload puts the export file in R2 under * imports/{integrator}/{ts} and writes the import_batches row, so Thursday's import runs SERVER SIDE * from stored bytes instead of a re upload, and the raw source stays around after extraction. Its * own multer instance (100MB) because the conversations export dwarfs the 20MB document limit, and * its own r2PutObject helper rather than a refactor of the four existing hand rolled upload blocks, * which stay untouched. Owner only to upload or delete; any teammate can read. * A batch REFUSES to delete once archive rows point at it, so a cleanup click can never take the * archive with it; drop the archive rows first, on purpose, with intent. * * BUILT FOR 500 INTEGRATORS, not for AO. Every table carries integrator_id and every lookup index * leads with it, so one integrator's archive can never make another's query slower. Archive reads * are paginated with a hard cap of 100 rows and are never unbounded. A batch is the unit of undo: * one bad run drops in one statement. Nothing here is AO shaped or Freshdesk shaped beyond a * `source` column, so the RingCentral transcripts and the Slack pull land in the same tables. * * 1.59.0 - Places autocomplete biases to the integrator's own area (portal only, NO dashboard change). * Fallout from 1.58.0 that had to be paid: when the browser called Google directly, Google read * the INTEGRATOR's IP and quietly weighted results near them. Proxied, Google reads Railway's * datacenter IP instead, so that implicit local signal was gone and a thin query like "3326 37th" * could rank an Alabama match against a Delano one. The replacement is better than what it * replaces because it is per INTEGRATOR rather than per browser: AO weights to the Twin Cities and * AVDCI weights to Chris Palmer's area no matter whose laptop or phone is typing, including a tech * on the road. locationBias (a 50km circle, the Places maximum) NOT locationRestriction, on purpose: * a bias reorders, it never hides, so an AO client with a cabin three hours north still comes up. * COORDINATES RESOLVE THEMSELVES. No dashboard change, no backfill, no setting for an integrator to * fill in: the first autocomplete after deploy geocodes the stored company address once through * Places searchText (same key, same API restriction, one call ever) and caches lat/lng on the * integrators row alongside the address string it came from. Change the company address in Settings * and the stored string stops matching, so it re-resolves on its own. An integrator with no company * address on file gets exactly the 1.58.0 behavior, unbiased, nothing breaks. A geocode failure is * cooled down 15 minutes in memory so a bad address cannot fire a lookup per keystroke, and bias is * ALWAYS optional: any failure path returns null and the autocomplete still answers. New columns * places_lat, places_lng, places_geo_addr, places_geo_at (idempotent ALTERs in initDb). * 1.58.0 - Places proxy: the Google Places key comes out of the browser (pairs with dashboard v1.60.8; * deploy this first). GitHub secret scanning flagged the key that has lived as a literal string * in dashboard.html since v1.27.0. Rotating it does not fix anything, because dashboard.html * ships to a PUBLIC GitHub Pages repo, so any key in that file is public by definition no matter * how it is referrer locked (a referrer header is one curl flag). The key now lives ONLY in the * GOOGLE_PLACES_KEY env var on this service. Two new routes, POST /api/places/autocomplete and * GET /api/places/details/:placeId, both requireAuth, so the Places quota now sits behind the * dashboard login instead of sitting on the open internet. The request shape is fixed server side * on purpose: the caller sends only the typed text and its session token, never a field mask and * never a region, so nobody can widen a proxied call into a more expensive Places surface. The * responses are trimmed to exactly the fields the dashboard renders. Uses global fetch, already * used elsewhere in this file, so there is no new dependency and package.json does not move. The * env read is .trim()ed, which is the parked env secret trim hardening applied where it now * matters (a trailing space in a Railway paste has already bitten this project twice, portal * 1.1.2 and the webhook R2 secret). No schema change. * 1.57.0 - Webhook ticket moments relay to push (pairs with webhook v9.22.0 + systemPrompt v9.13; deploy this * first). /api/notify now accepts an optional ticket_push block ({title, body, assignee_id}) riding * the same call as a ticket_marker row; the marker still broadcasts over SSE exactly as before, and * the block relays to sendPush under the existing new_ticket pref so the matrix is unchanged. Schema (idempotent): * push_subscriptions table (one row per device, endpoint unique) and integrator_users gains * push_prefs JSONB, push_mute_until, quiet_enabled, quiet_start, quiet_end. Defaults per Andy: client_message all, everything else off. ROOT CAUSE: the dashboard picker hands the portal naive wall time ("2026-06-12 14:00"), Postgres stores it in a naive TIMESTAMP, node-postgres reads it back as server-local, Railway runs UTC, so the value came back as 2pm UTC and every Central rendering shifted it to 9am. FIX: scheduled_at is now treated as the integrator's literal wall clock end to end. New wallClock() helper re-emits the stored value as a zone-free "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM" string (the Date's UTC fields ARE the stored wall time); applied on every route that returns scheduled_at (tickets board, conversation context, household detail, ticket update RETURNING) so the dashboard displays and round-trips the picked time with zero drift. formatScheduleDate() now formats those literal components (weekday computed at UTC, no zone conversion), so the #18 confirmation text says exactly the time the integrator picked. SETTING: integrators.timezone (VARCHAR 64, default America/Chicago) added in initDb, returned by GET /api/company, editable through PUT /api/company (owner only, validated against Intl). Nothing converts through it yet; it exists so every future instant-to-local rendering (morning email, reports) has one authoritative zone per integrator instead of a hardcode. Audit note: opened_at/created_at columns store UTC instants and render through the browser correctly, they are NOT shifted by this change; follow_ups.due_date is date-only and already component-parsed in the dashboard (fmtDueDate). Pairs with dashboard v1.18.0 (timezone select in Settings); portal deploys first and is safe alone. * 1.22.0 - Client-texts-first attach + phone lifecycle fixes (the Moitz incident, all three). (1) Household deactivate now cascades: removing a household flips its client_phones rows inactive in the same transaction, releasing the numbers (a number left active on a removed household silently blocked it from ever being added anywhere else, with no path to discover why). Reactivate restores phones mirroring the person-reactivate logic exactly: only numbers not now active elsewhere, only for people still active on the household, orphan rows included so the thread keeps resolving. (2) Add person adopts an orphan: when the duplicate-phone guard hits a row with NULL client_id on the SAME household (client-texts-first created the phone before the person existed), the new person adopts that row instead of getting a 409; the 409 stays for numbers owned by a real person or a different household. (3) New POST /api/conversations/:phone/attach turns an unknown texting number into a person in one shot: household_id present adds them to that home (last name defaults to the primary), absent creates a new household around them (last name required); either way the existing orphan phone row is ADOPTED, never duplicated, so opt-in status and conversation history ride along untouched; guards against numbers that already belong to a person. (4) GET /api/conversations now resolves the household name through the phone row too (COALESCE(cl.household_id, cp.household_id)) so an orphan number on a known home shows its household, and returns attached (boolean: a person owns this number) so the dashboard can badge unknown threads. The tickets table's tickets_status_check allowed only open/resolved/escalated, so every write of status 'scheduled' was rejected by the database with a 500 (both the Scheduled quick-status button and the dated Save path), even though the code was correct. initDb now drops and re-adds the constraint idempotently on every boot to allow open/scheduled/resolved/escalated/closed, so it can never silently regress. Added rememberMe session extension. * 1.0.1 - Fixed CORS and session cookie config. * 1.0.0 - Initial build. */ const express = require('express'); const Anthropic = require('@anthropic-ai/sdk'); const { S3Client, GetObjectCommand, DeleteObjectCommand } = require('@aws-sdk/client-s3'); const { getSignedUrl } = require('@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner'); const multer = require('multer'); const { Pool } = require('pg'); const bcrypt = require('bcrypt'); const session = require('express-session'); const PgSession = require('connect-pg-simple')(session); const Telnyx = require('telnyx'); const crypto = require('crypto'); const app = express(); app.set('trust proxy', 1); // 1.53.1: a full sales-order import (Blattner: 323 rows with accessories) exceeds the express // 100kb default, and the cross-origin 413 surfaced in the browser as 'Failed to fetch'. app.use(express.json({ limit: '10mb' })); app.use(express.urlencoded({ extended: false })); const ALLOWED_ORIGINS = [ 'https://restwerks.com', 'https://www.restwerks.com', 'http://127.0.0.1:5500', 'http://localhost:5500', 'http://localhost:3000' ]; app.use((req, res, next) => { const origin = req.headers.origin; if (origin && ALLOWED_ORIGINS.includes(origin)) { res.header('Access-Control-Allow-Origin', origin); } res.header('Access-Control-Allow-Credentials', 'true'); res.header('Access-Control-Allow-Headers', 'Content-Type, Authorization'); res.header('Access-Control-Allow-Methods', 'GET, POST, PUT, DELETE, OPTIONS'); // v1.21.0: let Chrome cache the preflight answer for two hours (its cap) instead of // re-asking before every call. Cuts dashboard request volume roughly in half. res.header('Access-Control-Max-Age', '7200'); if (req.method === 'OPTIONS') return res.sendStatus(200); next(); }); const pool = new Pool({ connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL, ssl: { rejectUnauthorized: false } }); const telnyx = new Telnyx(process.env.TELNYX_API_KEY); const anthropic = new Anthropic({ apiKey: process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }); const r2 = new S3Client({ region: 'auto', endpoint: `https://${process.env.R2_ACCOUNT_ID}.r2.cloudflarestorage.com`, credentials: { accessKeyId: process.env.R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID, secretAccessKey: process.env.R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY }, requestChecksumCalculation: 'WHEN_REQUIRED', responseChecksumValidation: 'WHEN_REQUIRED' }); const upload = multer({ storage: multer.memoryStorage(), limits: { fileSize: 20 * 1024 * 1024 } }); // ── v1.56.0: web push (PWA Slice B). Guarded so the portal always boots: a missing lib or // missing VAPID vars just logs push disabled and every sendPush becomes a no-op. ── let webpush = null; try { webpush = require('web-push'); if (process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY && process.env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY) { webpush.setVapidDetails( process.env.VAPID_SUBJECT || 'mailto:andy@restwerks.com', process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY, process.env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY ); console.log('[push] web push configured'); } else { webpush = null; console.log('[push] VAPID env vars not set; web push disabled'); } } catch (e) { webpush = null; console.log('[push] web-push lib not installed; web push disabled'); } // Defaults locked by Andy July 20 2026: client messages are the only event on out of the box. const PUSH_DEFAULTS = { client_message: 'all', // all | off takeover_handback: 'off', // all | off new_ticket: 'off', // all | mine | off ticket_assigned: 'off', // on | off (assigned to me) followup_assigned: 'off', // on | off (assigned to me) unknown_number: 'off' // on | off (unknown number texts in) }; function pushInQuietWindow(startHM, endHM, nowHM) { if (!startHM || !endHM || startHM === endHM) return false; if (startHM < endHM) return nowHM >= startHM && nowHM < endHM; return nowHM >= startHM || nowHM < endHM; // wraps midnight } // The per-user fan-out. sseNotify broadcasts at the INTEGRATOR level; push must be PER USER // (each person's devices, prefs, mute, quiet hours). Best effort by design: any failure logs // and never blocks the event that triggered it. A push service that reports the subscription // gone (404/410) gets its row deleted so dead devices self clean. async function sendPush(integratorId, kind, note, opts) { opts = opts || {}; if (!webpush) return; try { const subs = await pool.query( `SELECT s.id, s.endpoint, s.keys, s.user_id, u.push_prefs, u.push_mute_until, u.quiet_enabled, u.quiet_start, u.quiet_end FROM push_subscriptions s JOIN integrator_users u ON u.id = s.user_id AND u.integrator_id = s.integrator_id WHERE s.integrator_id = $1 AND u.active = true`, [integratorId] ); if (!subs.rows.length) return; const tzr = await pool.query('SELECT timezone FROM integrators WHERE id = $1', [integratorId]); const tz = (tzr.rows[0] && tzr.rows[0].timezone) || 'America/Chicago'; let nowHM = ''; try { nowHM = new Date().toLocaleTimeString('en-GB', { timeZone: tz, hour12: false, hour: '2-digit', minute: '2-digit' }); } catch (e) {} const now = new Date(); const payload = JSON.stringify({ title: note.title || 'RestWerks', body: (note.body || '').slice(0, 140), phone: note.phone || null, kind: kind }); for (const sub of subs.rows) { if (opts.excludeUserId && sub.user_id === opts.excludeUserId) continue; if (sub.push_mute_until && new Date(sub.push_mute_until) > now) continue; if (sub.quiet_enabled && nowHM && pushInQuietWindow(sub.quiet_start, sub.quiet_end, nowHM)) continue; const prefs = Object.assign({}, PUSH_DEFAULTS, sub.push_prefs || {}); let pass = false; if (kind === 'client_message') { pass = prefs.client_message === 'all' || (opts.unknown && prefs.unknown_number === 'on'); } else if (kind === 'takeover_handback') { pass = prefs.takeover_handback === 'all'; } else if (kind === 'new_ticket') { pass = prefs.new_ticket === 'all' || (prefs.new_ticket === 'mine' && opts.assigneeId && sub.user_id === opts.assigneeId) || (prefs.ticket_assigned === 'on' && opts.assigneeId && sub.user_id === opts.assigneeId); } else if (kind === 'ticket_assigned') { pass = prefs.ticket_assigned === 'on' && opts.assigneeId && sub.user_id === opts.assigneeId; } else if (kind === 'followup_assigned') { pass = prefs.followup_assigned === 'on' && opts.assigneeId && sub.user_id === opts.assigneeId; } if (!pass) continue; try { await webpush.sendNotification({ endpoint: sub.endpoint, keys: sub.keys }, payload); } catch (pushErr) { const code = pushErr.statusCode || 0; if (code === 404 || code === 410) { try { await pool.query('DELETE FROM push_subscriptions WHERE id = $1', [sub.id]); } catch (delErr) {} } else { console.error('[push] send failed:', code, pushErr.message); } } } } catch (err) { console.error('[push] fan-out error:', err.message); } } const sseClients = {}; function sseNotify(integratorId, event, data) { const clients = sseClients[integratorId] || []; const payload = 'event: ' + event + '\ndata: ' + JSON.stringify(data) + '\n\n'; clients.forEach(function(res) { try { res.write(payload); } catch(e) {} }); } app.use(session({ store: new PgSession({ pool, tableName: 'portal_sessions' }), secret: process.env.SESSION_SECRET || 'restwerks-dev-secret', resave: false, saveUninitialized: false, cookie: { secure: true, httpOnly: true, sameSite: 'none', maxAge: 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000 } })); async function requireAuth(req, res, next) { if (req.session && req.session.userId) { return next(); } const authHeader = req.headers['authorization']; if (authHeader && authHeader.startsWith('Bearer ')) { const token = authHeader.slice(7); try { const row = await getUserFromToken(token); if (row) { req.session.userId = row.user_id; req.session.integratorId = row.integrator_id; req.session.role = row.role; return next(); } } catch (err) { console.error('Token auth error:', err.message); } } return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Not authenticated' }); } // Owner-only guard. Runs after requireAuth, which sets req.session.role. function requireOwner(req, res, next) { if (req.session && req.session.role === 'owner') return next(); return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Owner access required' }); } // RestWerks super-admin guard (ops dash, v1.35.0). Runs after requireAuth. Reads the flag LIVE // from the DB per request rather than trusting the session, so revoking access (drop the email from // SUPERADMIN_EMAILS, redeploy) takes effect immediately even for an already-signed-in user. This is // the ONLY boundary that lets a route read across every integrator; everything else stays scoped to // req.session.integratorId. async function requireSuperadmin(req, res, next) { try { const r = await pool.query( 'SELECT is_superadmin FROM integrator_users WHERE id = $1', [req.session.userId] ); if (r.rows.length && r.rows[0].is_superadmin === true) return next(); } catch (err) { console.error('Superadmin check error:', err.message); } return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Not authorized' }); } async function initDb() { try { await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS portal_sessions ( sid VARCHAR NOT NULL COLLATE "default", sess JSON NOT NULL, expire TIMESTAMP(6) NOT NULL, CONSTRAINT portal_sessions_pkey PRIMARY KEY (sid) ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS iu_reset_token_idx ON integrator_users(reset_token)`); // ── Increment #4: team management. password_set distinguishes a real account from a pending invite. // DEFAULT true so every EXISTING user (who already chose a password) is settled. Invited users are // inserted with false explicitly, so a blanket default never marks a pending invite as active. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrator_users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS password_set BOOLEAN DEFAULT true`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrator_users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS cell_phone VARCHAR(20)`); // ── Role CHECK constraint self-heal (idempotent) ── // The original constraint allowed owner/admin/manager/employee and REJECTED 'member', // so member invites failed until it was fixed by hand in the Railway query tab. That hand // fix lives only in the current database; a fresh DB would re-block member invites. // This block makes the fix permanent: on every boot it drops ANY CHECK constraint on // integrator_users that references the role column (by whatever name it was created or // hand-renamed under, which we cannot assume), then re-adds the canonical owner/member rule. // The to_regclass guard makes it a safe no-op if the table does not exist yet. // Roadmap note: this will evolve into a capability-based model (named role presets + per-user // capabilities, e.g. billing scoped to a specific admin). owner/member is the forward-compatible // base; new tiers get added ABOVE member, never renaming these two. Widening this list is the // cheap part; the permission logic behind any new role is the real work and ships with that pass. await pool.query(` DO $$ DECLARE c text; BEGIN IF to_regclass('public.integrator_users') IS NULL THEN RETURN; END IF; FOR c IN SELECT con.conname FROM pg_constraint con JOIN pg_class rel ON rel.oid = con.conrelid JOIN pg_namespace ns ON ns.oid = rel.relnamespace WHERE rel.relname = 'integrator_users' AND ns.nspname = 'public' AND con.contype = 'c' AND pg_get_constraintdef(con.oid) ILIKE '%role%' LOOP EXECUTE format('ALTER TABLE integrator_users DROP CONSTRAINT %I', c); END LOOP; ALTER TABLE integrator_users ADD CONSTRAINT integrator_users_role_check CHECK (role IN ('owner','member')); END $$; `); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS status VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'preview'`); These make every // one of those an index hit so request bursts (SSE-driven reloads across many open // dashboards) stop queuing on the pool. conversations, tickets, and client_phones are // webhook-owned tables, so each is guarded with to_regclass: on a fresh database where // the webhook has not booted yet, the guard no-ops instead of failing the portal boot, // and the index lands on the next portal restart after the webhook creates the table. const perfIndexes = [ ['conversations', `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS conv_integrator_phone_created ON conversations(integrator_id, phone_number, created_at)`], ['tickets', `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS tickets_integrator_status ON tickets(integrator_id, status)`], ['tickets', `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS tickets_household ON tickets(household_id)`], ['client_phones', `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS client_phones_integrator_phone ON client_phones(integrator_id, phone_number)`], ['client_phones', `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS client_phones_household ON client_phones(household_id)`] ]; for (const [tbl, sql] of perfIndexes) { const exists = await pool.query(`SELECT to_regclass($1) AS t`, [tbl]); if (exists.rows[0] && exists.rows[0].t) await pool.query(sql); } // ── Household notes (Phase C2). The field is filled when there is something to fill it with, period. // ticket_type is OPEN TEXT on purpose so the taxonomy is never locked. Andi tags his best read // (truck roll, remote, firmware, other are seeded suggestions, not a constraint); a human can // correct it. That was the right fix for a fixed vocabulary. Statuses // are now ROWS in ticket_statuses that an integrator edits, so a CHECK constraint against // literal words is guaranteed to be wrong the first time a shop adds one, and it would fail // exactly the way 1.14.3 failed: a correct write rejected by the database with a 500 and no // wording anyone can read. Behavioral data lives with the PERSON (clients) // so it travels with them. Capture only: Andi writes these silently, nothing reads them back to // change his behavior yet. self_sufficiency and difficulty are 1-5; comm_voice is how they talk // and how Andi should answer; profile_notes is freeform. Message cadence is NOT stored, it is // computed live from the conversations table so it can never go stale. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS self_sufficiency INTEGER`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS difficulty INTEGER`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS comm_voice TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS profile_notes TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS profile_updated_at TIMESTAMP`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS profile_updated_by TEXT`); // ── Client type (v1.26.0) ── // One extensible per-person type from a RestWerks-curated list (Owner, Member, Caretaker, // House Manager, Special). The shared foundation later features read instead of each inventing // a flag: Owner carries household member-add authority; Special carries the response-window // rules. Nullable; existing rows stay unset until edited. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS client_type TEXT`); // ── Special coverage window (v1.27.0) ── // Wall-clock window (HH:MM, integrator timezone) a human covers a Special client. Andi stays // silent inside it and answers outside it. Only meaningful when client_type = 'Special'; // both null = no gate (a Special with no window set still gets Andi, never silenced by accident). await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS coverage_start TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE clients ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS coverage_end TEXT`); // ── Shared thread view stamps (v1.14.1) ── // Team-consistent unread and the in-thread "Seen by" line both read from here. One row per // user per thread, updated to NOW each time that user opens the thread. Shared across the team // (not per browser), which is what makes unread identical for everyone and lets a review item // clear once ANYONE on the team has looked. Keyed by phone so it survives household edits. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS thread_views ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, phone_number VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, user_id INTEGER NOT NULL, viewed_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(), UNIQUE (integrator_id, phone_number, user_id) ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS thread_views_lookup ON thread_views(integrator_id, phone_number)`); // ── Bubble color uniqueness (increment, June 7) ── // Colors are a per-person identity for fast visual attribution in a thread, so no two // users in one integrator may share an exact color. First free any existing duplicates // (keep the lowest id, null the rest; a nulled user gets a color on the next owner edit), // then add a partial unique index as the hard backstop. Nulls are allowed to repeat. await pool.query(` DO $$ DECLARE r RECORD; BEGIN IF to_regclass('public.integrator_users') IS NULL THEN RETURN; END IF; FOR r IN SELECT id FROM ( SELECT id, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY integrator_id, bubble_color ORDER BY id) AS rn FROM integrator_users WHERE bubble_color IS NOT NULL ) q WHERE q.rn > 1 LOOP UPDATE integrator_users SET bubble_color = NULL WHERE id = r.id; END LOOP; CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS iu_integrator_bubble_uniq ON integrator_users(integrator_id, bubble_color) WHERE bubble_color IS NOT NULL; END $$; `); // ── Ops dash boundary (v1.35.0) ─────────────────────────────────────────── // Two columns on integrator_users for the RestWerks-operator god view. last_login stamps the // last successful sign-in (the "who has actually been in" read). is_superadmin gates /api/ops/*. // Guarded with to_regclass because integrator_users is webhook/legacy-owned and may not exist on // a brand-new database where only the portal has booted. Super-admins are reconciled from the // SUPERADMIN_EMAILS env (comma-separated) on EVERY boot: matching active emails become true, every // other row becomes false, so the operator set is config-driven and self-correcting (no owner can // grant themselves the platform view through the UI, and dropping an email + redeploy revokes it). await pool.query(` DO $$ BEGIN IF to_regclass('public.integrator_users') IS NULL THEN RETURN; END IF; ALTER TABLE integrator_users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS last_login TIMESTAMP; ALTER TABLE integrator_users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS is_superadmin BOOLEAN DEFAULT false; END $$; `); { const supers = (process.env.SUPERADMIN_EMAILS || '') .split(',').map(s => s.trim().toLowerCase()).filter(Boolean); const tbl = await pool.query(`SELECT to_regclass('public.integrator_users') AS t`); if (tbl.rows[0].t) { if (supers.length) { await pool.query( `UPDATE integrator_users SET is_superadmin = (LOWER(email) = ANY($1))`, [supers] ); console.log(`Ops: ${supers.length} super-admin email(s) reconciled`); } else { await pool.query(`UPDATE integrator_users SET is_superadmin = false WHERE is_superadmin = true`); console.log('Ops: SUPERADMIN_EMAILS unset, no super-admins'); } } } // ── Pass 1 data foundation (v1.15.0, B12) ────────────────────────────────── // Household structured fields. Build for 500. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS control_system_type TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS isp_name TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS network_tier TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS priority_tier TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS lighting_system_type TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS camera_system_type TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS lock_system_type TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS security_manager_name TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS household_notes TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS remote_reboot_enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT false`); // #13 quick-facts Andi serves to opted-in household members (the major sell point). // Structured household-level fields. Capture/recall is already proven in the per-client // memory channel; these promote the two highest-value facts to the household so any member // can get them. Alarm codes are deliberately NOT stored. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS wifi_network TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS wifi_password TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS garage_code TEXT`); // #12 two-tier visibility. Nothing gates on it yet; the structure exists so gating wires later without a // migration. This is the client-vs-tech wall, not the across-the-household wall. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS field_visibility JSONB DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb`); // #4 per-number opt-in. Consent lives with the phone, not the conversation, because a // household has several numbers and each person consents for themself. Bootstrap flow is // client-texts-first: a number starts 'pending', flips to 'opted_in' when the person replies // YES to Andi's opt-in text (webhook, Pass 2), and 'opted_out' on STOP (the fuller STOP model // is A12). No backfill: existing numbers opt in through the same flow every real client uses. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE client_phones ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS opt_in_status VARCHAR(12) DEFAULT 'pending'`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE client_phones ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS opt_in_at TIMESTAMP`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE client_phones ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS opt_in_source TEXT`); // ── Structured address parts (v1.29.0) ── // Address autocomplete (Google Places) hands us each address already split into // street/city/state/zip at entry time. Applied to households and to the integrator's own address. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_street TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_city TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_state TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE households ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_zip TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_street TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_city TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_state TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS address_zip TEXT`); // ── Quick messages (v1.30.0, Cluster A slice) ── // Integrator-level reusable templates, surfaced in the conversation view as click-to-compose // (the body drops into the reply box; a human always sends, never auto-send). Add it on integrators // and drop the stray per-user column from v1.40.0 (it only ever held the default, so nothing is lost). await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS sidebar_logo_layout VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'logo_name'`); // 1.59.0: resolved coordinates for the company address, used to bias Places autocomplete to the // integrator's own area. places_geo_addr stores the address string they were derived FROM, which is // what makes a re-resolve automatic when the company address changes in Settings. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS places_lat DOUBLE PRECISION`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS places_lng DOUBLE PRECISION`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS places_geo_addr TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS places_geo_at TIMESTAMP`); await pool.query(`UPDATE integrators SET sidebar_logo_layout = 'logo_name' WHERE sidebar_logo_layout IS NULL`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrator_users DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS sidebar_logo_layout`); // ── Equipment data layer (v1.32.0). One row per device parsed from the as-sold sales order. // The sales order is the SOURCE OF TRUTH for what exists; this table holds it. room is the // verbatim as-sold label and never changes (the alias trail anchor); room_label is the // integrator rename Andi speaks, so the as-sold name is always recoverable.
