SLICE 1: PURELY ADDITIVE + a one-time backfill. // Nothing READS any of this yet (the equipment read/import/edit/render paths are untouched this // slice), so live behavior is identical. This lays the model that lets one household hold gear // from MANY source documents (AO AV order, AOL lighting order, change orders) across MULTIPLE // buildings, merged into one master room view. // // The shift: a ROOM stops being a bare text column on each device and becomes a real row that // OWNS a building, a level, a number (the stable cross-document key), a name, and name aliases. // Devices hang off a room via room_id and carry provenance (which document each row came from). // // household_buildings one per structure on a property (Main Cabin, Guest Cabin, Boathouse). A // room with building_id NULL is "unassigned" and reads as the implicit // single building until assigned. Buildings are REAL context Andi may see // (so he can disambiguate "the guest cabin or the main house?"). // household_rooms the first-class room. building_id + level are the editable grouping axes // (level stays HIDDEN from Andi, a viewing convenience; building is SHOWN // to Andi). room_number is the stable identifier a doc supplies (AOL labels // every room; AO will going forward). name is the display label; aliases // collects alternate names seen across documents so a merge never loses a // prior name. as_sold_name keeps the verbatim original anchor. // equipment.room_id links a device to its room (NULL for house/property-wide gear). // equipment.source_doc_id / source_label PROVENANCE: which uploaded document a row came from // (a SOFT ref to files.id, no hard FK, matching house style) plus a // denormalized label that survives if the file is later removed. The older // `source` column stays as the coarse type bucket. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS household_buildings ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, household_id INTEGER NOT NULL, name TEXT NOT NULL, sort_order INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(), updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS household_buildings_hh ON household_buildings(integrator_id, household_id, sort_order, id)`); await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS household_rooms ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, household_id INTEGER NOT NULL, building_id INTEGER, level TEXT, name TEXT, room_number TEXT, as_sold_name TEXT, aliases JSONB DEFAULT '[]', sort_order INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(), updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS household_rooms_hh ON household_rooms(integrator_id, household_id, building_id, sort_order, id)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS household_rooms_assold ON household_rooms(household_id, as_sold_name)`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE household_equipment ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS room_id INTEGER`); // 1.54.0: house-bucket gear (room_id null) still belongs to a structure; building_id keeps // Main House Security and Guest House Security apart. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE household_equipment ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS building_id INTEGER`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE household_equipment ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS source_doc_id INTEGER`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE household_equipment ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS source_label TEXT`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS household_equipment_room ON household_equipment(room_id)`); // 1.43.0 (slice 2): a room is tagged with the source document that created it, so two documents that // both name a "Kitchen" become two rooms (never silently merged; the slice-4 reconcile UI merges them // with a human confirm). NULL = the legacy/manual bucket the backfill and hand-add use. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE household_rooms ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS source_doc_id INTEGER`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS household_rooms_doc ON household_rooms(integrator_id, household_id, source_doc_id)`); // One-time backfill: rebuild rooms from each household's existing as-sold equipment, then link // every room-scoped device to its new room. Idempotent two ways: (1) rooms are only created for // a household that has NONE yet, (2) room_id is only set where it is still NULL. House and // property-wide rows (section_kind='house') intentionally get no room_id. building_id, level, // and room_number stay NULL here: the parser populates number going forward, and the integrator // assigns building/level in the grouping UI (a later slice). Re-running on every boot is a no-op // once linked. room name prefers the integrator rename (room_label) and falls back to the // as-sold room; sort_order follows first appearance in the original order. await pool.query(` INSERT INTO household_rooms (integrator_id, household_id, name, as_sold_name, sort_order) SELECT e.integrator_id, e.household_id, COALESCE(NULLIF(MAX(e.room_label), ''), e.room) AS name, e.room AS as_sold_name, MIN(e.sort_order) AS sort_order FROM household_equipment e WHERE e.section_kind = 'room' AND e.room IS NOT NULL AND e.room <> '' AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM household_rooms r WHERE r.integrator_id = e.integrator_id AND r.household_id = e.household_id) GROUP BY e.integrator_id, e.household_id, e.room `); await pool.query(` UPDATE household_equipment e SET room_id = r.id FROM household_rooms r WHERE e.room_id IS NULL AND e.section_kind = 'room' AND r.integrator_id = e.integrator_id AND r.household_id = e.household_id AND r.as_sold_name = e.room `); try { const _rc = await pool.query('SELECT count(*)::int AS n FROM household_rooms'); console.log('Equipment rooms after backfill:', _rc.rows[0].n); } catch (e) { /* self-report only; never block boot */ } // ── PERSON EMAIL (v1.60.0) ── // Mirrors client_phones. A person can carry more than one address; is_primary picks the one // shown and used first. client_id NULL is an EMAIL FIRST ORPHAN, the exact twin of the phone // first orphan client_phones already holds when a client texts before anyone entered them: // the address is known, the person is not yet, and a later edit adopts the row. // email_norm is the match key (lowercased, trimmed) and every lookup index leads with // integrator_id, so a lookup stays cheap with 500 integrators in the table. // NO UNIQUE CONSTRAINT on (integrator_id, email_norm), deliberately: unlike a phone number, an // address is legitimately shared, and a hard constraint would fail a real import row rather // than record it. The manual add and edit paths enforce the friendly duplicate 409 in code, // where the message can be worded; the import path records reality and parks the ambiguity. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS client_emails ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, household_id INTEGER, client_id INTEGER, email TEXT NOT NULL, email_norm TEXT NOT NULL, source TEXT DEFAULT 'manual', is_primary BOOLEAN DEFAULT false, active BOOLEAN DEFAULT true, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(), updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS client_emails_lookup ON client_emails(integrator_id, email_norm)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS client_emails_client ON client_emails(client_id)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS client_emails_hh ON client_emails(integrator_id, household_id)`); // ── IMPORT + ARCHIVE (v1.60.0) ── // An external export (Freshdesk first, RingCentral transcripts and Slack later) arrives as a // BATCH: one uploaded file, its stored bytes, its counts, and its status. Staging rows cascade // with the batch so a bad run drops in one statement. The archive does NOT cascade: it is the // body of record once applied and outlives the file that produced it. // // TWO RULES LIVE IN THE SHAPE OF THESE TABLES, not in the importer that fills them: // 1. Nothing here can grant consent. There is no opt in column and no textable flag anywhere // in this section, and the import path never writes client_phones. Freshdesk phone numbers // are reference text a person typed years ago, never validated by a delivered message, and // none of those clients consented to a text. Consent stays inbound first, always. // 2. Nothing here can guess. review_status defaults to 'pending' on every row, so an // unmatched or ambiguous contact parks in a review pile instead of attaching to the wrong // household. A wrong merge is far more expensive than a row a human has to look at. // raw JSONB keeps the source record verbatim so any later re derivation (a better matcher, a // second resolution extraction pass) never needs the original CSV again. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS import_batches ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'freshdesk', kind TEXT, r2_key TEXT, filename TEXT, mime_type TEXT, size_bytes BIGINT, row_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0, matched_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0, review_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0, error_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0, status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'uploaded', notes TEXT, stats JSONB DEFAULT '{}', created_by TEXT, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(), updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(), completed_at TIMESTAMP ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS import_batches_integrator ON import_batches(integrator_id, created_at DESC)`); // Staging contacts. Never a person record on its own: it holds the source row plus the MATCH // DECISION about it. match_key records WHICH key matched (email, phone, name) and // match_confidence how strongly, because the import matches on either key and ranks, while // live cross channel matching still needs both. Cascades with its batch. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS import_contacts ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, batch_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES import_batches(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'freshdesk', source_contact_id TEXT, name TEXT, first_name TEXT, last_name TEXT, company TEXT, email TEXT, email_norm TEXT, phone_raw TEXT, mobile_raw TEXT, phone_norm TEXT, is_active_src BOOLEAN, raw JSONB DEFAULT '{}', match_client_id INTEGER, match_household_id INTEGER, match_key TEXT, match_confidence NUMERIC(4,3), review_status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending', reviewed_by TEXT, reviewed_at TIMESTAMP, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS import_contacts_batch ON import_contacts(integrator_id, batch_id, id)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS import_contacts_email ON import_contacts(integrator_id, email_norm)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS import_contacts_review ON import_contacts(integrator_id, review_status)`); await pool.query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS import_contacts_src ON import_contacts(batch_id, source_contact_id) WHERE source_contact_id IS NOT NULL`); // The archive itself. Read only history, NOT working tickets: John's rule is that Freshdesk // history arrives as DATA, so nothing here ever writes into the tickets table and no client // ever sees these. household_id is the attach point every imported ticket must earn; until it // does, review_status keeps it parked. // UNIQUE (integrator_id, source, source_ticket_id) is what makes a re import idempotent: a // second run updates in place instead of doubling four years of history. // resolution_text is the extracted fix (Freshdesk has no resolution field, so it comes from the // trailing agent reply) and resolution_record_id is a soft link to the row it became in the // learning pool, so extraction can be re run without losing what already landed. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS archive_tickets ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, batch_id INTEGER, source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'freshdesk', source_ticket_id TEXT NOT NULL, subject TEXT, description TEXT, status_label TEXT, priority_label TEXT, ticket_type TEXT, requester_name TEXT, requester_email TEXT, requester_email_norm TEXT, requester_phone_norm TEXT, source_contact_id TEXT, custom_fields JSONB DEFAULT '{}', raw JSONB DEFAULT '{}', opened_at TIMESTAMP, updated_at_src TIMESTAMP, first_responded_at TIMESTAMP, resolved_at TIMESTAMP, closed_at TIMESTAMP, household_id INTEGER, client_id INTEGER, match_key TEXT, match_confidence NUMERIC(4,3), review_status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending', reviewed_by TEXT, reviewed_at TIMESTAMP, resolution_text TEXT, resolution_source TEXT, resolution_extracted_at TIMESTAMP, resolution_record_id INTEGER, note_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS archive_tickets_src ON archive_tickets(integrator_id, source, source_ticket_id)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS archive_tickets_hh ON archive_tickets(integrator_id, household_id, opened_at DESC)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS archive_tickets_review ON archive_tickets(integrator_id, review_status)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS archive_tickets_email ON archive_tickets(integrator_id, requester_email_norm)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS archive_tickets_batch ON archive_tickets(integrator_id, batch_id)`); // Every reply on an archived ticket, client and agent alike, in order. is_client separates the // 3,075 rows where the client wrote back from the agent side, which is what makes an issue and // fix pair extractable at all. noise flags the browser user agent blob Freshdesk writes as the // first note on every web form ticket: flagged rather than dropped, so the filter stays // inspectable and a bad filter is reversible without a re import. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS archive_notes ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, archive_ticket_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES archive_tickets(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'freshdesk', source_note_id TEXT, seq INTEGER DEFAULT 0, body TEXT, from_name TEXT, from_email TEXT, from_email_norm TEXT, is_client BOOLEAN DEFAULT false, is_private BOOLEAN DEFAULT false, noise BOOLEAN DEFAULT false, created_at_src TIMESTAMP, raw JSONB DEFAULT '{}', created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS archive_notes_ticket ON archive_notes(archive_ticket_id, seq, id)`); await pool.query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS archive_notes_src ON archive_notes(integrator_id, source, source_note_id) WHERE source_note_id IS NOT NULL`); // ── TICKET CLOCK (v1.64.0) ── // Response and resolution time, John's item 2. Structure lands now, the resolution // number fills in when the status model ships; the column exists so that is a wiring // job rather than another migration. // // WHY A TABLE AND NOT A SUBTRACTION: closed_at minus opened_at is exactly the number // John is complaining about. A ticket parked three weeks on a backordered part reads // as a three week resolution, which blames the shop for the supplier. One row per // status change, each carrying whether that status COUNTS, makes the clock a sum over // intervals and makes it auditable later instead of a number nobody can check. // // The three stop-the-clock statuses are not invented: Waiting on Client, Scheduled // With Client and Waiting on Parts all appear in AO's own Freshdesk export, so this // is the vocabulary the office already uses. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ticket_status_events ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, ticket_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES tickets(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, status TEXT NOT NULL, counts_toward_clock BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, entered_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(), entered_by TEXT, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_status_events_ticket ON ticket_status_events(ticket_id, entered_at, id)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_status_events_integrator ON ticket_status_events(integrator_id, entered_at)`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS first_response_at TIMESTAMP`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS first_response_seconds INTEGER`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS resolution_seconds INTEGER`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS clock_running_since TIMESTAMP`); // Backfill response time on every ticket that does not have one: the first HUMAN // message on that thread after the ticket opened. Idempotent by the IS NULL guard, so // this runs once for real and is a no-op on every boot after. Andi's own replies are // excluded on purpose: the question is when a PERSON got back to them. await pool.query(` UPDATE tickets t SET first_response_at = fr.at, first_response_seconds = GREATEST(0, EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (fr.at - t.opened_at))::int) FROM ( SELECT tk.id AS ticket_id, (SELECT c.created_at FROM conversations c WHERE c.integrator_id = tk.integrator_id AND c.phone_number = tk.phone_number AND c.role = 'human' AND c.created_at >= tk.opened_at ORDER BY c.created_at ASC LIMIT 1) AS at FROM tickets tk WHERE tk.first_response_at IS NULL AND tk.opened_at IS NOT NULL AND tk.phone_number IS NOT NULL ) fr WHERE t.id = fr.ticket_id AND fr.at IS NOT NULL AND t.first_response_at IS NULL `); // ── STATUS AND PRIORITY AS DATA (v1.68.0) ──────────────────────────────── // Two tables, same shape, one row per value per integrator. The important column is KIND, // because kind is what the product reasons about and label is only what the shop calls it. // Counts, badges, the resolution rate, the clock and every future Andi answer key on kind, // so renaming "Waiting on Parts" to "Parts on order" is a cosmetic change and can never // break a total. is_locked marks the four the product cannot be taught a new meaning for. // // KINDS, and this list is closed: // open the beginning. Clock runs. Locked. // scheduled owns scheduled_at. Locked. Clock toggle is the shop's call. // waiting any middle status. The ONLY kind an integrator can create. // resolved ended WITH a fix. Requires a note. Feeds learning. Defines the rate. Locked. // closed ended WITHOUT a fix. Requires a reason. Feeds nothing. Locked. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ticket_statuses ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, key TEXT NOT NULL, label TEXT NOT NULL, kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'waiting', color TEXT, sort_order INTEGER DEFAULT 0, counts_toward_clock BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, is_locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false, active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_statuses_key ON ticket_statuses(integrator_id, key)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_statuses_sort ON ticket_statuses(integrator_id, sort_order, id)`); await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ticket_priorities ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, key TEXT NOT NULL, label TEXT NOT NULL, rank INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 50, color TEXT, sort_order INTEGER DEFAULT 0, is_default BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false, active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_priorities_key ON ticket_priorities(integrator_id, key)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_priorities_sort ON ticket_priorities(integrator_id, sort_order, id)`); // priority is a KEY into ticket_priorities, deliberately not an integer, so renaming or // reordering the list never rewrites a ticket. closed_reason is why a ticket ended WITHOUT a // fix and is the counterpart to resolution_note; the two are never the same column because // one feeds the learning pool and the other must never reach it. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS priority TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS closed_reason TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS status_changed_at TIMESTAMP`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS tickets_status_idx ON tickets(integrator_id, status)`); // ── NUDGE FLAGS ON THE STATUS ROW (v1.71.0) ────────────────────────────── // Two independent booleans and an interval, defaulting OFF on every status including the ones // that already exist. A flag rather than a hardcoded key, because statuses are editable rows: // a shop can rename Waiting on Client or invent their own, and reading the literal key is the // same mistake as reading status by key instead of kind. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE ticket_statuses ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS nudge_client BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE ticket_statuses ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS nudge_team BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE ticket_statuses ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS nudge_client_hours INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 24`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE ticket_statuses ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS nudge_team_hours INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 24`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE ticket_statuses ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS nudge_max_stages INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 2`); // THE MASTER SWITCH, and it is FALSE for every existing integrator, not just new ones. This // feature introduced itself by texting a real client twice about a visit that was already on // the calendar, so it earns an explicit opt in from people who have already met it. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE integrators ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS nudges_enabled BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`); // ── ESCALATED IS RETIRED (v1.68.0) ─────────────────────────────────────── // It lived in the constraint and twelve places in this file, once in the dashboard, and never // in the webhook, because it was trying to be a status and a priority at the same time. The // real need behind it is priority. Rows move to open; they are live work, not an ending. await pool.query(`UPDATE tickets SET status = 'open' WHERE status = 'escalated'`); // ── SEED (v1.68.0) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────── // Every integrator that has none gets the six. Guarded per integrator by a NOT EXISTS on that // integrator's own rows rather than a global flag, so a new integrator seeds on its first boot // after signup and a shop that has DELETED down to a smaller list is never re-seeded behind // their back. The two waiting statuses are not invented: Waiting on Client and Waiting on // Parts both appear in AO's own Freshdesk export, so this is the vocabulary the office // already speaks. Scheduled counts toward the clock by default (a booked visit is the shop's // own time) and is the one seeded status whose toggle is genuinely a judgement call. await pool.query(` INSERT INTO ticket_statuses (integrator_id, key, label, kind, color, sort_order, counts_toward_clock, is_locked) SELECT i.id, v.key, v.label, v.kind, v.color, v.sort_order, v.counts, v.locked FROM integrators i CROSS JOIN (VALUES ('open', 'Open', 'open', '#cc4400', 10, true, true), ('scheduled', 'Scheduled', 'scheduled', '#7c3aed', 20, true, true), ('waiting_client', 'Waiting on Client', 'waiting', '#2D7DD2', 30, false, false), ('waiting_parts', 'Waiting on Parts', 'waiting', '#b8860b', 40, false, false), ('resolved', 'Resolved', 'resolved', '#1D9E75', 50, false, true), ('closed', 'Closed', 'closed', '#7a8290', 60, false, true) ) AS v(key, label, kind, color, sort_order, counts, locked) WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM ticket_statuses ts WHERE ts.integrator_id = i.id) ON CONFLICT (integrator_id, key) DO NOTHING `); // Priorities. rank is the machine-readable severity and sort_order is display order; they are // separate so a shop can reorder the picker without changing what "more urgent" means. Idempotent by the IS NULL guard. await pool.query(` UPDATE tickets t SET priority = p.key FROM ticket_priorities p WHERE p.integrator_id = t.integrator_id AND p.is_default = true AND t.priority IS NULL `); // ── THE CATEGORY TAXONOMY (v1.70.0) ────────────────────────────────────── // Eleven parents plus Other. Parents are the expensive change and children are cheap, and // the table is shaped around exactly that: a shop HIDES a parent it does not service and // SPLITS a child finer, and neither move touches a historical row or a ratio. There is no // route that adds or deletes a parent, because that is a migration and a real decision, // not a form. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ticket_categories ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, key TEXT NOT NULL, label TEXT NOT NULL, sort_order INTEGER DEFAULT 0, visible BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, is_locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); // A child is unique within its PARENT, never globally, which is what lets camera and // doorbell live under both Security and Surveillance as genuinely separate rows. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ticket_subcategories ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, category_key TEXT NOT NULL, key TEXT NOT NULL, label TEXT NOT NULL, sort_order INTEGER DEFAULT 0, visible BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, is_locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false, active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); nothing writes it today and nothing reads it. await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS category TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS subcategory TEXT`); await pool.query(`ALTER TABLE tickets ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS legacy_category TEXT`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS tickets_category_idx ON tickets(integrator_id, category)`); // The EXTRA subcategories. A distributed audio fault is amplifier and distribution at // once; the primary is on the ticket and the rest ride here. Never counted in a ratio. await pool.query(` CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ticket_subcategory_links ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, integrator_id INTEGER NOT NULL, ticket_id INTEGER NOT NULL, category_key TEXT NOT NULL, subcategory_key TEXT NOT NULL, created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW() ) `); await pool.query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_subcat_link_key ON ticket_subcategory_links(ticket_id, category_key, subcategory_key)`); await pool.query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ticket_subcat_link_tix ON ticket_subcategory_links(integrator_id, ticket_id)`); // ── TAXONOMY SEED (v1.70.0) ────────────────────────────────────────────── // Guarded per integrator by a NOT EXISTS on that integrator's own rows, the same shape the // status seed uses, so a shop that has HIDDEN half the list is never re-seeded behind their // back on the next deploy. Power supply is a child of nearly every parent, by Andy's call: // a dead supply presents as the device being dead and it is the cheapest fix in the house. // The Power parent stays house power infrastructure, which is a different fix, a different // tech and a different vendor. await pool.query(` INSERT INTO ticket_categories (integrator_id, key, label, sort_order) SELECT i.id, v.key, v.label, v.sort_order FROM integrators i CROSS JOIN (VALUES ('video', 'Video', 10), ('audio', 'Audio', 20), ('control', 'Control system', 30), ('lighting', 'Lighting control',40), ('shades', 'Shades', 50), ('network', 'Network', 60), ('security', 'Security', 70), ('surveillance', 'Surveillance', 80), ('access', 'Access control', 90), ('climate', 'Climate', 100), ('power', 'Power', 110), ('other', 'Other', 120) ) AS v(key, label, sort_order) WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM ticket_categories tc WHERE tc.integrator_id = i.id) ON CONFLICT (integrator_id, key) DO NOTHING `); // Screen is NOT a video issue: it is fabric and a motor, and when motorized it behaves like // a shade. Projector is its own child. Sonos is a Source, flagged for the equipment mapping // because a Sonos Amp or Port is an amplifier or distribution by function, so routing may // end up by SKU rather than brand name. Voice is a child of Control system and not a // parent: today it is a bolt on to Elan and Control4 and the failure is the integration. // Named now while it is small, because promoting a child to a parent later is the // expensive migration. Other has no children on purpose: Other climbing is a monitoring // signal that we named something wrong, and giving it children would hide that. await pool.query(` INSERT INTO ticket_subcategories (integrator_id, category_key, key) DO NOTHING `); // ── CLOCK BACKFILL (v1.68.0) ───────────────────────────────────────────── // ticket_status_events shipped empty in 1.64.0. Every ticket that has no event at all gets a // synthetic opening row at its opened_at, so the history is never blank and the clock has a // start. Guarded by NOT EXISTS, so this runs once for real and is a no-op on every boot after. await pool.query(` INSERT INTO ticket_status_events (integrator_id, ticket_id, status, counts_toward_clock, entered_at, entered_by) SELECT t.integrator_id, t.id, 'open', true, COALESCE(t.opened_at, NOW()), 'backfill' FROM tickets t WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM ticket_status_events e WHERE e.ticket_id = t.id) `); // A ticket that already ENDED gets its closing event too, plus the naive closed-minus-opened // number as its resolution time. That number is exactly the one John is complaining about, // and it is used HERE and only here, because for a ticket nobody was ever clocking it is the // only honest thing available. Everything opened from this build forward gets the real sum. await pool.query(` INSERT INTO ticket_status_events (integrator_id, ticket_id, status, counts_toward_clock, entered_at, entered_by) SELECT t.integrator_id, t.id, t.status, false, t.closed_at, 'backfill' FROM tickets t WHERE t.closed_at IS NOT NULL AND t.status IN ('resolved','closed') AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM ticket_status_events e WHERE e.ticket_id = t.id AND e.entered_by = 'backfill' AND e.status = t.status) `); await pool.query(` UPDATE tickets SET resolution_seconds = GREATEST(0, EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (closed_at - opened_at))::int), clock_running_since = NULL, status_changed_at = COALESCE(status_changed_at, closed_at) WHERE resolution_seconds IS NULL AND closed_at IS NOT NULL AND opened_at IS NOT NULL AND status IN ('resolved','closed') `); // A ticket still alive starts accumulating from the moment of this deploy rather than from // its opened_at. Backdating would hand John a pile of tickets that already read six weeks // old on day one, which is a number nobody can act on and nobody trusts. The clock is honest // from the day the clock exists. await pool.query(` UPDATE tickets SET resolution_seconds = COALESCE(resolution_seconds, 0), clock_running_since = COALESCE(clock_running_since, NOW()), status_changed_at = COALESCE(status_changed_at, opened_at, NOW()) WHERE status NOT IN ('resolved','closed') `); console.log('Portal database ready'); } catch (err) { console.error('initDb error:', err); } } // ─── TOKEN HELPERS ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── async function createToken(userId, integratorId, rememberMe) { const token = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex'); const days = rememberMe ? 30 : 7; const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + days * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000); await pool.query( `INSERT INTO auth_tokens (user_id, integrator_id, token, expires_at) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)`, [userId, integratorId, token, expiresAt] ); return token; } async function getUserFromToken(token) { if (!token) return null; const result = await pool.query( `SELECT at.user_id, at.integrator_id, u.role FROM auth_tokens at JOIN integrator_users u ON u.id = at.user_id WHERE at.token = $1 AND at.expires_at > NOW()`, [token] ); return result.rows[0] || null; } // ─── AUTH HELPERS ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── const PORTAL_PUBLIC_URL = process.env.PORTAL_PUBLIC_URL || 'https://restwerks-portal-production.up.railway.app'; const DASHBOARD_URL = process.env.DASHBOARD_URL || 'https://restwerks.com/dashboard'; function validatePassword(pw) { if (typeof pw !== 'string' || pw.length < 10) return 'Password must be at least 10 characters'; if (!/[a-z]/.test(pw)) return 'Password must include a lowercase letter'; if (!/[A-Z]/.test(pw)) return 'Password must include an uppercase letter'; if (!/[0-9]/.test(pw)) return 'Password must include a number'; if (!/[^A-Za-z0-9]/.test(pw)) return 'Password must include a special character'; return null; } function validEmail(email) { return typeof email === 'string' && /^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$/.test(email); } // v1.60.0: the stored match key for an address. Lowercase and trim only, nothing clever: no plus // tag stripping and no dot folding, because those rules are provider specific and a wrong // normalization silently merges two different people. Matching is allowed to miss, never to merge. function normalizeEmail(raw) { if (raw == null) return null; const t = String(raw).trim().toLowerCase(); return t ? t : null; } // v1.60.0: read a person's emails onto rows already fetched. One query for the whole page rather // than one per person, so a household with a dozen people costs the same as one with two. // activeOnly is true for client facing reads (the conversation rail) and false for the edit // surface, which has to show a removed address so it can be restored. async function attachClientEmails(integratorId, rows, activeOnly) { if (!rows || !rows.length) return rows; const ids = rows.map(r => r.client_id).filter(id => id != null); if (!ids.length) { rows.forEach(r => { r.email = null; r.emails = []; }); return rows; } const q = await pool.query( `SELECT id AS email_id, client_id, email, email_norm, is_primary, active, source FROM client_emails WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND client_id = ANY($2::int[]) ${activeOnly ? 'AND active = true' : ''} ORDER BY is_primary DESC, id ASC`, [integratorId, ids] ); const byClient = {}; for (const e of q.rows) { (byClient[e.client_id] = byClient[e.client_id] || []).push(e); } for (const r of rows) { const list = byClient[r.client_id] || []; r.emails = list; const primary = list.find(e => e.active) || null; r.email = primary ? primary.email : null; } return rows; } // v1.60.0: write one address onto a person inside an open transaction. An empty string CLEARS // (deactivates) the way the phone path does, so the two person fields behave identically. // Adopts an orphan row (client_id NULL) on the same household rather than creating a duplicate, // exactly as the phone path adopts a client texts first row. async function setClientEmail(db, integratorId, householdId, clientId, rawEmail) { const norm = normalizeEmail(rawEmail); if (!norm) { await db.query('UPDATE client_emails SET active = false, updated_at = NOW() WHERE client_id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [clientId, integratorId]); return; } const mine = await db.query( 'SELECT id FROM client_emails WHERE client_id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2 ORDER BY is_primary DESC, id ASC LIMIT 1', [clientId, integratorId] ); if (mine.rows.length) { await db.query( 'UPDATE client_emails SET email = $1, email_norm = $2, household_id = $3, active = true, is_primary = true, updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = $4 AND integrator_id = $5', [String(rawEmail).trim(), norm, householdId, mine.rows[0].id, integratorId] ); return; } const orphan = await db.query( 'SELECT id FROM client_emails WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND email_norm = $2 AND client_id IS NULL AND household_id = $3 AND active = true LIMIT 1', [integratorId, norm, householdId] ); if (orphan.rows.length) { await db.query( 'UPDATE client_emails SET client_id = $1, email = $2, is_primary = true, updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = $3 AND integrator_id = $4', [clientId, String(rawEmail).trim(), orphan.rows[0].id, integratorId] ); return; } await db.query( 'INSERT INTO client_emails (integrator_id, household_id, client_id, email, email_norm, source, is_primary, active) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,true,true)', [integratorId, householdId, clientId, String(rawEmail).trim(), norm, 'manual'] ); } // v1.60.0: the duplicate guard for the MANUAL paths only. The database carries no unique // constraint on purpose (a shared address is real and an import must be able to record it), so the // friendly 409 that the phone path gives lives here, where it can be worded, and applies only // where a human is typing. Returns true when the address already belongs to somebody else. async function emailTakenByOther(db, integratorId, norm, exceptClientId) { if (!norm) return false; const r = await db.query( `SELECT 1 FROM client_emails WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND email_norm = $2 AND active = true AND client_id IS NOT NULL AND client_id <> COALESCE($3, -1) LIMIT 1`, [integratorId, norm, exceptClientId || null] ); return r.rows.length > 0; } // v1.60.0: PUT bytes to R2. A helper this time rather than a fifth copy of the hand rolled SigV4 // block: the four existing upload paths are deliberately left untouched (a working upload is not // worth the regression risk on a sprint night), and every new upload uses this. async function r2PutObject(key, buffer, mimeType) { const bucket = process.env.R2_BUCKET_NAME; const accountId = process.env.R2_ACCOUNT_ID; const accessKeyId = process.env.R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID; const secretAccessKey = process.env.R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY; const region = 'auto'; const service = 's3'; const host = `${bucket}.${accountId}.r2.cloudflarestorage.com`; const now = new Date(); const amzDate = now.toISOString().replace(/[:-]|\.\d{3}/g, '').slice(0, 15) + 'Z'; const dateStamp = amzDate.slice(0, 8); const bodyHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(buffer).digest('hex'); const ct = mimeType || 'application/octet-stream'; const canonicalHeaders = `content-type:${ct}\nhost:${host}\nx-amz-content-sha256:${bodyHash}\nx-amz-date:${amzDate}\n`; const signedHeaders = 'content-type;host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date'; const canonicalRequest = `PUT\n/${key}\n\n${canonicalHeaders}\n${signedHeaders}\n${bodyHash}`; const credentialScope = `${dateStamp}/${region}/${service}/aws4_request`; const stringToSign = `AWS4-HMAC-SHA256\n${amzDate}\n${credentialScope}\n${crypto.createHash('sha256').update(canonicalRequest).digest('hex')}`; const kDate = crypto.createHmac('sha256', `AWS4${secretAccessKey}`).update(dateStamp).digest(); const kRegion = crypto.createHmac('sha256', kDate).update(region).digest(); const kService = crypto.createHmac('sha256', kRegion).update(service).digest(); const signingKey = crypto.createHmac('sha256', kService).update('aws4_request').digest(); const signature = crypto.createHmac('sha256', signingKey).update(stringToSign).digest('hex'); const authorization = `AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=${accessKeyId}/${credentialScope}, SignedHeaders=${signedHeaders}, Signature=${signature}`; const uploadRes = await fetch(`https://${host}/${key}`, { method: 'PUT', headers: { 'Content-Type': ct, 'x-amz-date': amzDate, 'x-amz-content-sha256': bodyHash, 'Authorization': authorization }, body: buffer }); if (!uploadRes.ok) { const errText = await uploadRes.text(); throw new Error(`R2 upload failed: ${uploadRes.status} ${errText}`); } return key; } // v1.60.0: an export file is far larger than a household document (the AO conversations export is // 7,704 rows), so imports get their own multer instance at 100MB. The 20MB `upload` used by every // existing route is untouched. const uploadImport = multer({ storage: multer.memoryStorage(), limits: { fileSize: 100 * 1024 * 1024 } }); // Microsoft Graph mail, dependency-free. Mirrors the webhook sender (andi@restwerks.com). // Requires Node 18+ for global fetch. If unavailable the call throws and callers treat email as best-effort. async function getGraphToken() { const tenant = process.env.AZURE_TENANT_ID; const body = new URLSearchParams({ client_id: process.env.AZURE_CLIENT_ID, client_secret: process.env.AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET, scope: 'https://graph.microsoft.com/.default', grant_type: 'client_credentials' }); const r = await fetch(`https://login.microsoftonline.com/${tenant}/oauth2/v2.0/token`, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded' }, body: body.toString() }); const j = await r.json(); if (!j.access_token) throw new Error('Graph token failed: ' + JSON.stringify(j)); return j.access_token; } async function sendMail(toEmail, subject, htmlBody) { const token = await getGraphToken(); const r = await fetch('https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/users/andi@restwerks.com/sendMail', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + token, 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ message: { subject, body: { contentType: 'HTML', content: htmlBody }, toRecipients: [{ emailAddress: { address: toEmail } }] }, saveToSentItems: false }) }); if (!r.ok) throw new Error('sendMail failed: ' + r.status + ' ' + (await r.text())); } function verificationEmailHtml(firstName, link) { return `

Hi ${firstName || 'there'},

Confirm your email to finish setting up your RestWerks account. You are already logged in and can explore the dashboard now. Confirming your email is required before you go live.

Confirm my email

Or paste this link into your browser:
${link}

This link expires in 3 days.

`; } async function sendVerificationEmail(email, firstName, token) { const link = `${PORTAL_PUBLIC_URL}/auth/verify?token=${token}`; await sendMail(email, 'Confirm your RestWerks email', verificationEmailHtml(firstName, link)); } // Set-password email, used for both teammate invites and forgot-password resets. The link lands on the // dashboard with a token; the dashboard shows a set-password screen that posts to /auth/set-password. function setPasswordEmailHtml(firstName, link, isInvite, companyName) { const intro = isInvite ? `You have been added to the ${companyName || 'team'} dashboard on RestWerks. Set a password to get in.` : `We got a request to reset your RestWerks password. Set a new one with the button below. If this was not you, you can ignore this email.`; const expiry = isInvite ? 'This link expires in 7 days.' : 'This link expires in 2 hours.'; const btn = isInvite ? 'Set my password' : 'Reset my password'; return `

Hi ${firstName || 'there'},

${intro}

${btn}

Or paste this link into your browser:
${link}

${expiry}

`; } async function sendSetPasswordEmail(email, firstName, token, isInvite, companyName) { const link = `${DASHBOARD_URL}?setpw=${token}`; const subject = isInvite ? 'You have been added to RestWerks' : 'Reset your RestWerks password'; await sendMail(email, subject, setPasswordEmailHtml(firstName, link, isInvite, companyName)); } // Per-user chat bubble colors. Pick the first unused color so teammates stay visually distinct. const BUBBLE_PALETTE = ['#2D7DD2','#7B5EA7','#1D9E75','#E08600','#C0392B','#0E8A8A','#D6336C','#5C7CFA']; // Curated palette first (legible, distinct, on-brand). Once those are all taken within an // integrator, generate a fresh color guaranteed not equal to any in use, walking the hue // circle. "Never the exact same" is guaranteed; visual distinctness is best-effort at the // far end. usedColors should include removed users too, since a color stays reserved to a person. function pickBubbleColor(usedColors) { const used = (usedColors || []).filter(Boolean).map(c => c.toLowerCase()); for (const c of BUBBLE_PALETTE) { if (used.indexOf(c.toLowerCase()) === -1) return c; } // Palette exhausted: step around the hue circle until we land on an unused hex. for (let i = 0; i < 360; i++) { const hue = (i * 47) % 360; // 47 is coprime with 360, so it visits many distinct hues const hex = hslToHex(hue, 55, 45); if (used.indexOf(hex.toLowerCase()) === -1) return hex; } // Extreme fallback (every generated hue somehow taken): nudge lightness. for (let l = 30; l <= 70; l += 2) { const hex = hslToHex((used.length * 47) % 360, 55, l); if (used.indexOf(hex.toLowerCase()) === -1) return hex; } return hslToHex((used.length * 47) % 360, 60, 50); } function hslToHex(h, s, l) { s /= 100; l /= 100; const k = n => (n + h / 30) % 12; const a = s * Math.min(l, 1 - l); const f = n => l - a * Math.max(-1, Math.min(k(n) - 3, Math.min(9 - k(n), 1))); const toHex = x => Math.round(255 * x).toString(16).padStart(2, '0'); return '#' + toHex(f(0)) + toHex(f(8)) + toHex(f(4)); } // ─── PUSH (v1.56.0, PWA Slice B) ───────────────────────────────────────────── // Self serve, any authenticated user. Literal routes only, no param collisions. app.get('/api/push/vapid-key', requireAuth, (req, res) => { if (!webpush || !process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY) return res.status(503).json({ error: 'Push is not configured yet' }); res.json({ key: process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY }); }); app.post('/api/push/subscribe', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const userId = req.session.userId; const sub = req.body && req.body.subscription; if (!sub || !sub.endpoint || !sub.keys) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'subscription required' }); const label = (req.body.device_label || '').slice(0, 120) || null; try { // Upsert by endpoint: a re-subscribe from the same device replaces its row (and follows the // device if a user logs in on a teammate's phone, which is the right ownership). await pool.query( `INSERT INTO push_subscriptions (integrator_id, user_id, endpoint, keys, device_label) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5) ON CONFLICT (endpoint) DO UPDATE SET integrator_id = $1, user_id = $2, keys = $4, device_label = $5`, [integratorId, userId, sub.endpoint, JSON.stringify(sub.keys), label] ); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Push subscribe error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.post('/api/push/unsubscribe', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const endpoint = req.body && req.body.endpoint; if (!endpoint) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'endpoint required' }); try { await pool.query('DELETE FROM push_subscriptions WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND endpoint = $2', [integratorId, endpoint]); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Push unsubscribe error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.get('/api/push/prefs', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { try { const r = await pool.query( 'SELECT push_prefs, push_mute_until, quiet_enabled, quiet_start, quiet_end FROM integrator_users WHERE id = $1', [req.session.userId] ); const u = r.rows[0] || {}; res.json({ prefs: Object.assign({}, PUSH_DEFAULTS, u.push_prefs || {}), mute_until: u.push_mute_until || null, quiet_enabled: !!u.quiet_enabled, quiet_start: u.quiet_start || null, quiet_end: u.quiet_end || null, configured: !!webpush }); } catch (err) { console.error('Push prefs read error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.put('/api/push/prefs', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const b = req.body || {}; try { const sets = []; const params = []; let i = 1; if (b.prefs && typeof b.prefs === 'object') { // Only known keys with known values ever land in the record. const clean = {}; for (const k of Object.keys(PUSH_DEFAULTS)) { if (typeof b.prefs[k] === 'string') clean[k] = b.prefs[k]; } sets.push(`push_prefs = $${i++}`); params.push(JSON.stringify(clean)); } if (b.mute_until !== undefined) { sets.push(`push_mute_until = $${i++}`); params.push(b.mute_until || null); } if (b.quiet_enabled !== undefined) { sets.push(`quiet_enabled = $${i++}`); params.push(!!b.quiet_enabled); } if (b.quiet_start !== undefined) { sets.push(`quiet_start = $${i++}`); params.push((b.quiet_start || '').slice(0, 5) || null); } if (b.quiet_end !== undefined) { sets.push(`quiet_end = $${i++}`); params.push((b.quiet_end || '').slice(0, 5) || null); } if (!sets.length) return res.json({ ok: true }); params.push(req.session.userId); await pool.query(`UPDATE integrator_users SET ${sets.join(', ')} WHERE id = $${i}`, params); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Push prefs save error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── PLACES PROXY (v1.58.0) ─────────────────────────────────────────── // The Google Places key used to sit as a literal string in dashboard.html, which ships to a public // GitHub Pages repo, so GitHub's secret scanner flagged it. Any browser side key is public by // definition; the referrer lock is a speed bump, not a wall. The key now lives only in the // GOOGLE_PLACES_KEY env var on this service and the dashboard calls these two routes instead. // Both are requireAuth, so the quota sits behind the login and cannot be burned by anyone reading // the repo. The request shape is fixed HERE on purpose: the caller sends the typed text and its // session token and nothing else, so a caller cannot widen the query into a more expensive Places // surface by passing its own field mask or region. Responses are trimmed to exactly what the // dashboard renders. If the env var is missing the routes answer 503 and the dashboard falls back // to plain typing, which is the same graceful degradation the typeahead has always had. const PLACES_TIMEOUT_MS = 6000; function placesKey() { // .trim() deliberately: a trailing space on a Railway paste has bitten this project twice. return (process.env.GOOGLE_PLACES_KEY || '').trim(); } async function placesFetch(url, options) { const ctrl = new AbortController(); const timer = setTimeout(() => ctrl.abort(), PLACES_TIMEOUT_MS); try { return await fetch(url, Object.assign({}, options, { signal: ctrl.signal })); } finally { clearTimeout(timer); } } // Bias radius in meters. 50000 is the Places maximum for an autocomplete circle, and it is a BIAS // (reorder) not a RESTRICTION (hide), so an address well outside the circle still comes back, just // lower. That matters: an AO client with a lake place up north must stay findable. const PLACES_BIAS_RADIUS_M = 50000; const PLACES_BIAS_TTL_MS = 15 * 60 * 1000; const placesBiasCache = new Map(); // integratorId -> { bias, addr, at } function companyAddressString(row) { if (!row) return ''; const parts = [row.address_street, row.address_city, row.address_state, row.address_zip] .map(v => (v || '').trim()).filter(Boolean); if (parts.length) return parts.join(', '); return (row.address || '').trim(); } // Resolve the integrator's company address to coordinates ONCE and remember them. Returns null for // anything that does not resolve, and null always means "no bias", never an error to the caller. async function getIntegratorBias(integratorId, key) { if (!integratorId) return null; const cached = placesBiasCache.get(integratorId); if (cached && (Date.now() - cached.at) < PLACES_BIAS_TTL_MS) return cached.bias; let row; try { const r = await pool.query( `SELECT address, address_street, address_city, address_state, address_zip, places_lat, places_lng, places_geo_addr FROM integrators WHERE id = $1`, [integratorId] ); row = r.rows[0]; } catch (err) { console.error('Places bias read error:', err.message); return null; } if (!row) return null; const addr = companyAddressString(row); if (!addr) { placesBiasCache.set(integratorId, { bias: null, addr: '', at: Date.now() }); return null; } // Stored coordinates are good only while they still describe the CURRENT company address. if (row.places_lat != null && row.places_lng != null && (row.places_geo_addr || '') === addr) { const bias = { latitude: Number(row.places_lat), longitude: Number(row.places_lng) }; placesBiasCache.set(integratorId, { bias, addr, at: Date.now() }); return bias; } // One geocode, then it is remembered. Same key, same Places API restriction. try { const r = await placesFetch('https://places.googleapis.com/v1/places:searchText', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', 'X-Goog-Api-Key': key, 'X-Goog-FieldMask': 'places.location' }, body: JSON.stringify({ textQuery: addr, maxResultCount: 1 }) }); if (!r.ok) throw new Error('searchText ' + r.status); const data = await r.json(); const loc = data.places && data.places[0] && data.places[0].location; if (!loc || typeof loc.latitude !== 'number' || typeof loc.longitude !== 'number') throw new Error('no location'); await pool.query( `UPDATE integrators SET places_lat = $1, places_lng = $2, places_geo_addr = $3, places_geo_at = NOW() WHERE id = $4`, [loc.latitude, loc.longitude, addr, integratorId] ); const bias = { latitude: loc.latitude, longitude: loc.longitude }; placesBiasCache.set(integratorId, { bias, addr, at: Date.now() }); console.log(`[places] bias resolved for integrator ${integratorId}: ${addr}`); return bias; } catch (err) { // Cool down so an unresolvable company address cannot fire a lookup on every keystroke. console.error('Places bias geocode failed:', err.message); placesBiasCache.set(integratorId, { bias: null, addr, at: Date.now() }); return null; } } app.post('/api/places/autocomplete', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const key = placesKey(); if (!key) return res.status(503).json({ error: 'Places not configured' }); const body = req.body || {}; const input = String(body.input == null ? '' : body.input).trim().slice(0, 200); const sessionToken = String(body.sessionToken == null ? '' : body.sessionToken).slice(0, 120); if (input.length < 4) return res.json({ suggestions: [] }); try { // Bias is best effort and always optional: no company address, or a geocode that did not resolve, // simply means an unbiased search rather than a failed one. const bias = await getIntegratorBias(req.session && req.session.integratorId, key); const payload = { input, sessionToken, includedRegionCodes: ['us'] }; if (bias) { payload.locationBias = { circle: { center: bias, radius: PLACES_BIAS_RADIUS_M } }; } const r = await placesFetch('https://places.googleapis.com/v1/places:autocomplete', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', 'X-Goog-Api-Key': key }, body: JSON.stringify(payload) }); if (!r.ok) { const text = await r.text().catch(() => ''); console.error('Places autocomplete upstream', r.status, text.slice(0, 300)); return res.status(502).json({ error: 'Places lookup failed' }); } const data = await r.json(); const suggestions = (data.suggestions || []) .filter(sg => sg && sg.placePrediction) .slice(0, 5) .map(sg => { const p = sg.placePrediction; const sf = p.structuredFormat || {}; return { placeId: p.placeId || '', main: (sf.mainText && sf.mainText.text) || (p.text && p.text.text) || '', secondary: (sf.secondaryText && sf.secondaryText.text) || '' }; }) .filter(sg => sg.placeId && sg.main); res.json({ suggestions }); } catch (err) { console.error('Places autocomplete error:', err.message); res.status(502).json({ error: 'Places lookup failed' }); } }); app.get('/api/places/details/:placeId', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const key = placesKey(); if (!key) return res.status(503).json({ error: 'Places not configured' }); const placeId = String(req.params.placeId || '').slice(0, 400); const sessionToken = String(req.query.sessionToken || '').slice(0, 120); if (!placeId) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'placeId required' }); try { const url = 'https://places.googleapis.com/v1/places/' + encodeURIComponent(placeId) + (sessionToken ? '?sessionToken=' + encodeURIComponent(sessionToken) : ''); const r = await placesFetch(url, { headers: { 'X-Goog-Api-Key': key, 'X-Goog-FieldMask': 'addressComponents,formattedAddress' } }); if (!r.ok) { const text = await r.text().catch(() => ''); console.error('Places details upstream', r.status, text.slice(0, 300)); return res.status(502).json({ error: 'Places lookup failed' }); } const place = await r.json(); // Field names match Google's on purpose so the dashboard's parseAddressParts is unchanged. res.json({ addressComponents: place.addressComponents || [], formattedAddress: place.formattedAddress || '' }); } catch (err) { console.error('Places details error:', err.message); res.status(502).json({ error: 'Places lookup failed' }); } }); // ─── HEALTH ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.get('/health', (req, res) => { res.json({ status: 'ok', service: 'restwerks-portal', version: '1.71.0' }); }); // ─── AUTH ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.post('/auth/login', async (req, res) => { const { email, password } = req.body; if (!email || !password) { return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Email and password required' }); } try { const result = await pool.query( `SELECT u.*, i.name as integrator_name, i.telnyx_number, i.brand_color as integrator_brand_color, i.status as integrator_status, i.setup_completed as integrator_setup_completed FROM integrator_users u JOIN integrators i ON i.id = u.integrator_id WHERE u.email = $1 AND u.active = true`, [email.toLowerCase().trim()] ); if (!result.rows.length) { return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Invalid email or password' }); } const user = result.rows[0]; const match = await bcrypt.compare(password, user.password_hash); if (!match) { return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Invalid email or password' }); } req.session.userId = user.id; req.session.integratorId = user.integrator_id; req.session.role = user.role; // Ops dash (v1.35.0): stamp the sign-in. Fire-and-forget so a logging hiccup never blocks login. pool.query('UPDATE integrator_users SET last_login = NOW() WHERE id = $1', [user.id]) .catch(e => console.error('last_login stamp error:', e.message)); if (req.body.rememberMe) { req.session.cookie.maxAge = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000; } const authToken = await createToken(user.id, user.integrator_id, req.body.rememberMe); res.json({ id: user.id, first_name: user.first_name, last_name: user.last_name, email: user.email, role: user.role, bubble_color: user.bubble_color, cell_phone: user.cell_phone, integrator_id: user.integrator_id, integrator_name: user.integrator_name, telnyx_number: user.telnyx_number, auth_token: authToken, integrator_brand_color: user.integrator_brand_color, status: user.integrator_status, email_verified: user.email_verified, setup_completed: user.integrator_setup_completed }); } catch (err) { console.error('Login error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.post('/auth/logout', (req, res) => { req.session.destroy(); res.json({ success: true }); }); app.get('/auth/me', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { try { const result = await pool.query( `SELECT u.*, i.name as integrator_name, i.telnyx_number, i.brand_color as integrator_brand_color, i.status as integrator_status, i.setup_completed as integrator_setup_completed FROM integrators i JOIN integrator_users u ON u.integrator_id = i.id WHERE u.id = $1`, [req.session.userId] ); } catch (err) { console.error('Me error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── SIGNUP (self-serve) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.post('/auth/signup', async (req, res) => { const { company_name, first_name, last_name, email, password } = req.body; if (!company_name || !first_name || !last_name || !email || !password) { return res.status(400).json({ error: 'All fields are required' }); } if (!validEmail(email)) { return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Enter a valid email address' }); if (exists.rows.length) { return res.status(409).json({ error: 'An account with that email already exists' }); } const passwordHash = await bcrypt.hash(password, 10); const verificationToken = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex'); A missing email never blocks the account. try { await sendVerificationEmail(cleanEmail, first_name.trim(), verificationToken); } catch (mailErr) { console.error('Verification email failed (account still created):', mailErr.message); res.json({ id: userId, first_name: first_name.trim(), last_name: last_name.trim(), email: cleanEmail, role: 'owner', bubble_color: '#2D7DD2', integrator_id: integratorId, integrator_name: company_name.trim(), telnyx_number: null, auth_token: authToken, integrator_brand_color: '#3B5168', status: 'preview', email_verified: false, setup_completed: false }); } catch (err) { try { await client.query('ROLLBACK'); } catch (e) {} console.error('Signup error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } finally { client.release(); } }); app.get('/auth/verify', async (req, res) => { const token = req.query.token; try { if (!token) return res.redirect(`${DASHBOARD_URL}?verified=0`); const result = await pool.query( `UPDATE integrator_users SET email_verified = true, verification_token = NULL, verification_expires = NULL WHERE verification_token = $1 AND verification_expires > NOW() RETURNING id`, [token] ); if (!result.rows.length) return res.redirect(`${DASHBOARD_URL}?verified=0`); return res.redirect(`${DASHBOARD_URL}?verified=1`); } catch (err) { console.error('Verify error:', err); return res.redirect(`${DASHBOARD_URL}?verified=0`); } }); app.post('/auth/resend-verification', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { try { const u = await pool.query( 'SELECT email, first_name, email_verified FROM integrator_users WHERE id = $1', [req.session.userId] ); if (!u.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'User not found' }); if (u.rows[0].email_verified) return res.json({ already_verified: true }); const verificationToken = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex'); const verificationExpires = new Date(Date.now() + 3 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000); await pool.query( 'UPDATE integrator_users SET verification_token = $1, verification_expires = $2 WHERE id = $3', [verificationToken, verificationExpires, req.session.userId] ); await sendVerificationEmail(u.rows[0].email, u.rows[0].first_name, verificationToken); res.json({ sent: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Resend verification error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Could not send verification email' }); } }); // ─── STATS ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.get('/api/stats', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; try { const conversations = await pool.query( `SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT phone_number) as total FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1`, [integratorId] ); const tickets = await pool.query( // v1.68.0: counts key on KIND. The old version compared the status column to five literal // words, so the first custom status a shop added would have dropped those tickets out of // every total on this page with no error anywhere. escalated_tickets is kept in the // response and returns 0: the status is retired, and removing the key would break a // dashboard that still reads it before its own half deploys. `SELECT COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE kind = 'open') as open_tickets, COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE kind = 'scheduled') as scheduled_tickets, COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE kind = 'waiting') as waiting_tickets, COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE kind IN ('open','scheduled','waiting')) as active_tickets, COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE kind = 'resolved') as resolved_tickets, COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE kind = 'closed') as closed_tickets, 0 as escalated_tickets FROM ( SELECT ${KIND_SQL} AS kind FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.integrator_id = $1 ) q`, [integratorId] ); } catch (err) { console.error('Stats error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── RESOLVED (v1.10.0) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────── // The logged-in user's display name, for stamping who resolved or flagged something. async function actorName(req) { try { const r = await pool.query('SELECT first_name, last_name FROM integrator_users WHERE id = $1', [req.session.userId]); if (r.rows.length) return `${r.rows[0].first_name || ''} ${r.rows[0].last_name || ''}`.trim() || 'You'; } catch (e) {} return 'You'; } // Recent conversations Andi summarized, parsed from the role=summary rows the morning // cron and handoff writer drop into each thread. Read-only cards for the Resolved tab. app.get('/api/resolved', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; try { const rows = await pool.query( `SELECT DISTINCT ON (c.phone_number) c.phone_number, c.message, c.created_at, cl.first_name, cl.last_name, h.name AS household_name FROM conversations c LEFT JOIN client_phones cp ON cp.phone_number = c.phone_number AND cp.integrator_id = c.integrator_id LEFT JOIN clients cl ON cl.id = cp.client_id LEFT JOIN households h ON h.id = cl.household_id WHERE c.integrator_id = $1 AND c.role = 'summary' ORDER BY c.phone_number, c.created_at DESC`, [integratorId] ); const out = rows.rows.map((r) => { let parsed = {}; try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.message) || {}; } catch (e) { parsed = {}; } const name = (r.first_name || r.last_name) ? `${r.first_name || ''} ${r.last_name || ''}`.trim() : (r.household_name || r.phone_number); return { phone_number: r.phone_number, name, household_name: r.household_name || null, issue: parsed.issue || null, andi_action: parsed.andi_action || null, client_mindset: parsed.client_mindset || null, follow_up_note: parsed.follow_up_note || null, created_at: r.created_at }; }).sort((a, b) => new Date(b.created_at) - new Date(a.created_at)); res.json(out); } catch (err) { console.error('Resolved error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── FOLLOW UPS (v1.10.0) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.get('/api/follow-ups', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; } }); // ─── TEAM (lightweight, for assignee pickers) ───────────────────────────────── // Any authenticated user can see the active team to assign work. Editable on the client side. Best-effort: returns an // empty draft if anything fails so the human can just type. app.post('/api/draft-issue', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const phone = (req.body && req.body.phone) ? decodeURIComponent(req.body.phone) : null; if (!phone) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'phone required' }); try { const msgs = await pool.query( `SELECT role, message FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 AND role IN ('user','assistant','human') ORDER BY created_at ASC`, [integratorId, phone] ); if (!msgs.rows.length) return res.json({ issue: '' }); const transcript = msgs.rows.map(m => `${m.role === 'user' ? 'Client' : (m.role === 'human' ? 'Team' : 'Andi')}: ${m.message}`).join('\n'); const response = await anthropic.messages.create({ model: 'claude-sonnet-4-5', max_tokens: 150, system: 'You write a short issue summary for a support ticket from a client conversation. Reply with one or two plain sentences describing the issue and what is still needed. No preamble, no labels, no quotes. Never use dashes.', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: `Transcript:\n${transcript}` }] }); const issue = (response.content[0] && response.content[0].text ? response.content[0].text : '').trim(); res.json({ issue }); } catch (err) { console.error('Draft issue error:', err.message); res.json({ issue: '' }); } }); // ─── DRAFT SYNOPSIS (v1.12.0) ───────────────────────────────────────────────── // At ticket close, Andi drafts the ticket-relevant context (what the issue was and how the // conversation went), NOT the fix. The human writes the actual resolution and closes. Best // effort: empty draft on failure so the human can just type. app.post('/api/draft-synopsis', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const phone = (req.body && req.body.phone) ? decodeURIComponent(req.body.phone) : null; const issue = (req.body && req.body.issue) ? String(req.body.issue).trim() : ''; if (!phone) return res.json({ synopsis: '' }); try { const msgs = await pool.query( `SELECT role, message FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 AND role IN ('user','assistant','human') ORDER BY created_at ASC`, [integratorId, phone] ); if (!msgs.rows.length) return res.json({ synopsis: '' }); const transcript = msgs.rows.map(m => `${m.role === 'user' ? 'Client' : (m.role === 'human' ? 'Team' : 'Andi')}: ${m.message}`).join('\n'); const focus = issue ? `This conversation may cover more than one topic. Summarize ONLY the part that relates to this specific ticket: "${issue}". Ignore unrelated issues in the thread.` : 'Summarize the issue this conversation was about.'; const response = await anthropic.messages.create({ model: 'claude-sonnet-4-5', max_tokens: 180, system: `You are writing the context portion of a resolution record for a support ticket. ${focus} In two or three plain sentences, describe what the issue was and how the conversation went. Do NOT invent or state the fix or what a technician did; that is written separately by a person. Use plain language: say processor, never "hub". No preamble, no labels, no quotes. Never use dashes.`, messages: [{ role: 'user', content: `Conversation:\n${transcript}` }] }); const synopsis = (response.content[0] && response.content[0].text ? response.content[0].text : '').trim(); res.json({ synopsis }); } catch (err) { console.error('Draft synopsis error:', err.message); res.json({ synopsis: '' }); } }); // ─── CONVERSATIONS ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.get('/api/conversations', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; try { // Bug B fix: the preview message, its role, and the sender name and color // all come from ONE row (the latest displayable message), so the label // can never describe a different row than the text. Handling state for the // Andi pill and unread mark comes from the human and handback markers, the // same math the thread detail view uses. const threads = await pool.query( `WITH base AS ( SELECT phone_number, MAX(created_at) AS last_at_all, COUNT(*) AS message_count FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 GROUP BY phone_number ), preview AS ( SELECT DISTINCT ON (phone_number) phone_number, message, role, sender_name, bubble_color, created_at FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND role IN ('user','assistant','human') ORDER BY phone_number, created_at DESC ), markers AS ( SELECT phone_number, MAX(created_at) FILTER (WHERE role = 'human') AS last_human_at, MAX(created_at) FILTER (WHERE role = 'assistant') AS last_assistant_at, MAX(created_at) FILTER (WHERE role = 'handback') AS last_handback_at FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 GROUP BY phone_number ), views AS ( SELECT phone_number, MAX(viewed_at) AS last_viewed_any FROM thread_views WHERE integrator_id = $1 GROUP BY phone_number ) SELECT b.phone_number, cl.first_name, cl.last_name, cl.client_type, cl.coverage_start, cl.coverage_end, (SELECT timezone FROM integrators WHERE id = $1) AS integrator_timezone, h.name AS household_name, (cp.client_id IS NOT NULL) AS attached, COALESCE(p.created_at, b.last_at_all) AS last_message_at, b.message_count, p.message AS last_message, p.role AS preview_role, p.sender_name AS last_sender_name, p.bubble_color AS last_sender_color, m.last_human_at, m.last_assistant_at, m.last_handback_at, v.last_viewed_any FROM base b LEFT JOIN preview p ON p.phone_number = b.phone_number LEFT JOIN markers m ON m.phone_number = b.phone_number LEFT JOIN views v ON v.phone_number = b.phone_number LEFT JOIN client_phones cp ON cp.phone_number = b.phone_number AND cp.integrator_id = $1 LEFT JOIN clients cl ON cl.id = cp.client_id LEFT JOIN households h ON h.id = COALESCE(cl.household_id, cp.household_id) ORDER BY last_message_at DESC`, [integratorId] ); res.json(threads.rows); } catch (err) { console.error('Conversations error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.get('/api/thread', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; Powers the in-thread line. const msgs = messages.rows; const lastAt = msgs.length ? msgs[msgs.length - 1].created_at : null; let viewers = []; if (lastAt) { const vr = await pool.query( `SELECT tv.user_id, tv.viewed_at, u.first_name, u.last_name, u.bubble_color FROM thread_views tv JOIN integrator_users u ON u.id = tv.user_id WHERE tv.integrator_id = $1 AND tv.phone_number = $2 AND tv.viewed_at >= $3 ORDER BY tv.viewed_at ASC`, [integratorId, phone, lastAt] ); viewers = vr.rows.map(function (v) { const fn = (v.first_name || '').trim(); const ln = (v.last_name || '').trim(); const initials = ((fn ? fn[0] : '') + (ln ? ln[0] : '')).toUpperCase() || '?'; return { user_id: v.user_id, name: (fn + ' ' + ln).trim() || 'Teammate', initials: initials, bubble_color: v.bubble_color || '#2D7DD2', viewed_at: v.viewed_at }; }); } res.json({ messages: msgs, clientName, householdName, viewers, client_type: row?.client_type || null, coverage_start: row?.coverage_start || null, coverage_end: row?.coverage_end || null, integrator_timezone: row?.integrator_timezone || null }); } catch (err) { console.error('Thread error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Record that the current user opened this thread (shared, team-wide). Clears a review-type unread // for everyone and feeds the "Seen by" line. Broadcasts a 'viewed' event so any open thread and the // conversation list update live as teammates look. app.post('/api/conversations/:phone/view', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const userId = req.session.userId; const phone = decodeURIComponent(req.params.phone); try { await pool.query( `INSERT INTO thread_views (integrator_id, phone_number, user_id, viewed_at) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, NOW()) ON CONFLICT (integrator_id, phone_number, user_id) DO UPDATE SET viewed_at = NOW()`, [integratorId, phone, userId] ); const u = await pool.query('SELECT first_name, last_name, bubble_color FROM integrator_users WHERE id = $1', [userId]); const r = u.rows[0] || {}; const fn = (r.first_name || '').trim(), ln = (r.last_name || '').trim(); const initials = ((fn ? fn[0] : '') + (ln ? ln[0] : '')).toUpperCase() || '?'; sseNotify(integratorId, 'viewed', { phone_number: phone, user_id: userId, name: (fn + ' ' + ln).trim() || 'Teammate', initials: initials, bubble_color: r.bubble_color || '#2D7DD2', viewed_at: new Date().toISOString() }); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Thread view error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.get('/api/conversations/:phone', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const phone = decodeURIComponent(req.params.phone); try { const messages = await pool.query( `SELECT role, message, created_at, sender_name, bubble_color FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 ORDER BY created_at ASC`, [integratorId, phone] ); res.json(messages.rows); } catch (err) { console.error('Thread error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Rail context for the conversation view (items 6 + 7). The texting person's behavioral read rides along for the rail too. app.get('/api/conversations/:phone/context', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const phone = decodeURIComponent(req.params.phone); const client = cr.rows[0] || null; let tickets = [], followUps = []; if (household) { const tr = await pool.query( `SELECT t.id, t.summary, t.status, t.ticket_type, t.scheduled_at, t.opened_at, t.closed_at, t.resolution_note, t.resolved_by, t.created_by, t.assigned_to, (u.first_name || ' ' || u.last_name) AS assignee_name, COALESCE((SELECT json_agg(json_build_object('id', ti.id, 'body', ti.body, 'source', ti.source, 'created_at', ti.created_at) ORDER BY ti.created_at, ti.id) FROM ticket_issues ti WHERE ti.ticket_id = t.id), '[]'::json) AS issues FROM tickets t LEFT JOIN integrator_users u ON u.id = t.assigned_to AND u.integrator_id = t.integrator_id WHERE t.household_id = $1 AND t.integrator_id = $2 ORDER BY t.opened_at DESC NULLS LAST, t.id DESC LIMIT 25`, [household.id, integratorId] ); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // v1.22.0: attach a texting number to a household. The client-texts-first flow creates a phone // row (and a whole conversation) before anyone is entered in the portal; this is the one-shot // path that turns that unknown number into a person. Two modes: household_id present adds the // person to that home; absent creates a new household around them. Either way the existing // orphan phone row is ADOPTED (not duplicated), so the opt-in status and the conversation // history keyed by this number ride along untouched. app.post('/api/conversations/:phone/attach', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const phone = normalizePhone(decodeURIComponent(req.params.phone)); if (!phone) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'A valid phone number is required' }); const b = req.body || {}; const first = (b.first_name || '').trim(); if (!first) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'First name is required' }); const relationship = (b.relationship || '').trim() || null; const householdId = b.household_id ? parseInt(b.household_id, 10) : null; const db = await pool.connect(); try { // The number must not already belong to a person. const existing = await db.query( 'SELECT id, client_id, household_id, active FROM client_phones WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 ORDER BY active DESC, id ASC LIMIT 1', [integratorId, phone] ); const row = existing.rows[0] || null; if (row && row.client_id !== null && row.active) { db.release(); return res.status(409).json({ error: 'That number already belongs to a person on a household' }); } let targetHouseholdId = householdId; let last = (b.last_name || '').trim(); await db.query('BEGIN'); if (targetHouseholdId) { const hr = await db.query('SELECT id FROM households WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [targetHouseholdId, integratorId]); if (!hr.rows.length) { await db.query('ROLLBACK'); db.release(); return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Household not found' }); } if (!last) { const ln = await db.query('SELECT last_name FROM clients WHERE household_id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2 AND active = true ORDER BY id ASC LIMIT 1', [targetHouseholdId, integratorId]); last = ln.rows.length ? ln.rows[0].last_name : ''; } if (!last) { await db.query('ROLLBACK'); db.release(); return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Last name is required' }); } } else { if (!last) { await db.query('ROLLBACK'); db.release(); return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Last name is required for a new household' }); } const label = buildHouseholdLabel({ first, last }, null); const hh = await db.query( `INSERT INTO households (integrator_id, name, project_name, address, address_street, address_city, address_state, address_zip) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6, $7, $8) RETURNING id`, [integratorId, label, (b.project_name || '').trim() || null, (b.address || '').trim() || null, (b.address_street || '').trim() || null, (b.address_city || '').trim() || null, (b.address_state || '').trim() || null, (b.address_zip || '').trim() || null] ); try { const result = await pool.query( `SELECT h.*, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.household_id = h.id AND t.integrator_id = h.integrator_id AND ${KIND_SQL} = 'open') AS open_ticket_count, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.household_id = h.id AND t.integrator_id = h.integrator_id AND ${KIND_SQL} = 'scheduled') AS scheduled_ticket_count, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.household_id = h.id AND t.integrator_id = h.integrator_id AND ${KIND_SQL} = 'waiting') AS waiting_ticket_count, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM follow_ups f WHERE f.household_id = h.id AND f.integrator_id = h.integrator_id AND f.status = 'pending') AS open_followup_count, array_agg(json_build_object( 'phone', cp.phone_number, 'client_id', cp.client_id, 'first_name', cl.first_name, 'last_name', cl.last_name, 'relationship', cl.relationship, 'client_type', cl.client_type, 'opt_in_status', cp.opt_in_status )) FILTER (WHERE cp.phone_number IS NOT NULL) as people FROM households h LEFT JOIN client_phones cp ON cp.household_id = h.id LEFT JOIN clients cl ON cl.id = cp.client_id WHERE h.integrator_id = $1 GROUP BY h.id ORDER BY h.name ASC`, [integratorId] );
