A status holding tickets // would need a merge flow (move them where?) and that is a real design, not a confirm dialog. // Refusing with the count is more useful than refusing with a rule, because it tells the owner // exactly what to do first. app.delete('/api/ticket-statuses/:id', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad status id' }); try { const cur = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM ticket_statuses WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!cur.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Status not found' }); const row = cur.rows[0]; if (row.is_locked) return res.status(400).json({ error: row.label + ' cannot be removed' }); const held = await pool.query('SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND status = $2', [integratorId, row.key]); if (held.rows[0].n > 0) { return res.status(400).json({ error: held.rows[0].n + ' ticket' + (held.rows[0].n === 1 ? ' is' : 's are') + ' in ' + row.label + '. Move them first.' }); } await pool.query('DELETE FROM ticket_statuses WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket status delete error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ── Priorities, same shape ─────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.post('/api/ticket-priorities', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const label = String((req.body || {}).label || '').trim(); if (!label) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Give the priority a name' }); const key = vocabKey(label); if (!key) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Give the priority a name with letters or numbers in it' }); const rank = Number.isFinite(Number((req.body || {}).rank)) ? Math.max(0, Math.min(100, parseInt((req.body || {}).rank, 10))) : 50; try { const v = await ensureTicketVocab(integratorId); if (v.priorities.some(function (r) { return r.key === key; })) { return res.status(409).json({ error: 'You already have a priority called that' }); } const maxSort = v.priorities.reduce(function (m, r) { return Math.max(m, r.sort_order || 0); }, 0); const color = String((req.body || {}).color || '').trim() || '#2D7DD2'; const r = await pool.query( `INSERT INTO ticket_priorities (integrator_id, key, label, rank, color, sort_order, is_default) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,false) RETURNING *`, [integratorId, key, label, rank, color, maxSort + 10] ); res.json({ ok: true, priority: r.rows[0] }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket priority create error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.post('/api/ticket-priorities/reorder', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const order = Array.isArray((req.body || {}).order) ? req.body.order : null; if (!order) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Send the new order' }); try { for (let idx = 0; idx < order.length; idx++) { await pool.query('UPDATE ticket_priorities SET sort_order = $1 WHERE id = $2 AND integrator_id = $3', [(idx + 1) * 10, parseInt(order[idx], 10), integratorId]); } const v = await ensureTicketVocab(integratorId); res.json({ ok: true, priorities: v.priorities }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket priority reorder error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.put('/api/ticket-priorities/:id', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad priority id' }); const b = req.body || {}; try { const cur = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM ticket_priorities WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!cur.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Priority not found' }); const sets = []; const params = []; let i = 1; if (typeof b.label === 'string' && b.label.trim()) { sets.push(`label = $${i++}`); params.push(b.label.trim()); } if (typeof b.color === 'string') { sets.push(`color = $${i++}`); params.push(b.color.trim() || null); } if (b.rank !== undefined && Number.isFinite(Number(b.rank))) { sets.push(`rank = $${i++}`); params.push(Math.max(0, Math.min(100, parseInt(b.rank, 10)))); } if (b.is_default === true) { // Exactly one default, always. Cleared across the whole list first so a failed second write // can never leave a shop with none, which would put new tickets back to a null priority. await pool.query('UPDATE ticket_priorities SET is_default = false WHERE integrator_id = $1', [integratorId]); sets.push(`is_default = true`); } if (!sets.length) return res.json({ ok: true, priority: cur.rows[0] }); params.push(id, integratorId); const r = await pool.query( `UPDATE ticket_priorities SET ${sets.join(', ')} WHERE id = $${i++} AND integrator_id = $${i} RETURNING *`, params ); res.json({ ok: true, priority: r.rows[0] }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket priority update error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.delete('/api/ticket-priorities/:id', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad priority id' }); try { const cur = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM ticket_priorities WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!cur.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Priority not found' }); const row = cur.rows[0]; if (row.is_default) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'That is the default priority. Make another one the default first.' }); const held = await pool.query('SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND priority = $2', [integratorId, row.key]); if (held.rows[0].n > 0) { return res.status(400).json({ error: held.rows[0].n + ' ticket' + (held.rows[0].n === 1 ? ' is' : 's are') + ' set to ' + row.label + '. Move them first.' }); } await pool.query('DELETE FROM ticket_priorities WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket priority delete error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Remove an ADDED issue. The headline summary is not a ticket_issues row, so it can never be // reached here; only issues that were appended are removable. app.delete('/api/tickets/:id/issues/:issueId', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const { id, issueId } = req.params; try { const r = await pool.query( 'DELETE FROM ticket_issues WHERE id = $1 AND ticket_id = $2 AND integrator_id = $3 RETURNING id', [issueId, id, integratorId] ); if (!r.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Issue not found' }); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket issue delete error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── CATEGORY TAXONOMY ROUTES (v1.70.0) ─────────────────────────────────────── // Owner only, and deliberately NARROWER than the status and priority routes. There is no add // and no delete for a PARENT: the eleven plus Other are fixed, and a parent that appears or // disappears makes every prior ratio dishonest. What a shop can do is hide what it does not // service and split a child finer. // Hide or show a parent, or rename it. Hiding is not deleting: tickets already carrying that // category keep it and keep counting, because the alternative is a total that changes when // somebody toggles a checkbox. app.put('/api/ticket-categories/:id', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad category id' }); const { label, visible } = req.body; try { const cur = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM ticket_categories WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!cur.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Category not found' }); const sets = [], vals = []; let i = 1; if (typeof label === 'string' && label.trim()) { sets.push('label = $' + i++); vals.push(label.trim().slice(0, 60)); } if (typeof visible === 'boolean') { sets.push('visible = $' + i++); vals.push(visible); } if (!sets.length) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Nothing to change' }); vals.push(id, integratorId); const r = await pool.query( 'UPDATE ticket_categories SET ' + sets.join(', ') + ' WHERE id = $' + i++ + ' AND integrator_id = $' + i + ' RETURNING *', vals ); res.json(r.rows[0]); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket category update error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Add a child under an existing parent. This is the SPLIT path and it is the only growth this // taxonomy has. The parent must already exist, which is what stops a typo minting a twelfth // parent through the back door. app.post('/api/ticket-subcategories', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const { category_key, label } = req.body; const clean = String(label || '').trim(); if (!clean) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Name is required' }); if (!category_key) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Category is required' }); try { const parent = await pool.query( 'SELECT 1 FROM ticket_categories WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND key = $2 AND active = true', [integratorId, category_key] ); if (!parent.rows.length) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'That category does not exist' }); const key = vocabKey(clean); if (!key) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Name needs at least one letter or number' }); const maxRow = await pool.query( 'SELECT COALESCE(MAX(sort_order), 0) AS m FROM ticket_subcategories WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND category_key = $2', [integratorId, category_key] ); const r = await pool.query( `INSERT INTO ticket_subcategories (integrator_id, category_key, key, label, sort_order, is_locked) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, false) ON CONFLICT (integrator_id, category_key, key) DO NOTHING RETURNING *`, [integratorId, category_key, key, clean.slice(0, 60), Number(maxRow.rows[0].m) + 10] ); if (!r.rows.length) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'That already exists under this category' }); res.json(r.rows[0]); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket subcategory create error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); app.put('/api/ticket-subcategories/:id', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad subcategory id' }); const { label, visible } = req.body; try { const cur = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM ticket_subcategories WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!cur.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Subcategory not found' }); const sets = [], vals = []; let i = 1; if (typeof label === 'string' && label.trim()) { sets.push('label = $' + i++); vals.push(label.trim().slice(0, 60)); } if (typeof visible === 'boolean') { sets.push('visible = $' + i++); vals.push(visible); } if (!sets.length) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Nothing to change' }); vals.push(id, integratorId); const r = await pool.query( 'UPDATE ticket_subcategories SET ' + sets.join(', ') + ' WHERE id = $' + i++ + ' AND integrator_id = $' + i + ' RETURNING *', vals ); res.json(r.rows[0]); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket subcategory update error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Delete only a CUSTOM child holding zero tickets, and refuse WITH THE COUNT, because a merge // flow is a real design and not a confirm dialog. A seeded child is hidden, never deleted: it // is part of the shared vocabulary every shop's numbers are compared against. app.delete('/api/ticket-subcategories/:id', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad subcategory id' }); try { const cur = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM ticket_subcategories WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!cur.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Subcategory not found' }); const row = cur.rows[0]; if (row.is_locked) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'That one is part of the standard list. You can hide it instead.' }); const held = await pool.query( `SELECT (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND category = $2 AND subcategory = $3) + (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ticket_subcategory_links WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND category_key = $2 AND subcategory_key = $3) AS n`, [integratorId, row.category_key, row.key] ); const n = Number(held.rows[0].n); if (n > 0) { return res.status(400).json({ error: n + ' ticket' + (n === 1 ? ' is' : 's are') + ' tagged ' + row.label + '. Move them first.' }); } await pool.query('DELETE FROM ticket_subcategories WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ticket subcategory delete error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── SEND MESSAGE ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.post('/api/send', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const userId = req.session.userId; const { to, message } = req.body; if (!to || !message) { return res.status(400).json({ error: 'to and message required' }); } try { const integratorResult = await pool.query( 'SELECT telnyx_number FROM integrators WHERE id = $1', [integratorId] ); if (!integratorResult.rows.length) { return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Integrator not found' }); } const fromNumber = integratorResult.rows[0].telnyx_number; const userResult = await pool.query( 'SELECT first_name, last_name, bubble_color FROM integrator_users WHERE id = $1', [userId] ); const user = userResult.rows[0]; const senderName = user ? `${user.first_name} ${user.last_name}`.trim() : 'Team'; const bubbleColor = user ? user.bubble_color : '#1D9E75'; // Check if this is first human reply before writing anything const lastMarkerResult = await pool.query( `SELECT role, created_at FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 AND role IN ('summary', 'handback') ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1`, [integratorId, to] ); const lastMarkerTime = lastMarkerResult.rows[0]?.created_at || new Date(0); const humanSinceMarker = await pool.query( `SELECT id FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 AND role = 'human' AND created_at > $3`, [integratorId, to, lastMarkerTime] ); const isFirstHumanSinceMarker = humanSinceMarker.rows.length === 0; // If first human reply, write summary first so it sits before the human message in DB if (isFirstHumanSinceMarker) { const integratorResult2 = await pool.query('SELECT name FROM integrators WHERE id = $1', [integratorId]); const intName = integratorResult2.rows[0]?.name || 'RestWerks'; await writeHandoffSummary(integratorId, to, intName); // v1.56.0: the first human reply IS the takeover moment. Push the team (per prefs), // never the person who just replied. Best effort. sendPush(integratorId, 'takeover_handback', { title: senderName + ' took over a thread', body: message, phone: to }, { excludeUserId: req.session.userId }).catch(function(){}); } // Now insert human message and send via Telnyx await pool.query( `INSERT INTO conversations (integrator_id, phone_number, role, message, sender_name, bubble_color) VALUES ($1, $2, 'human', $3, $4, $5)`, [integratorId, to, message, senderName, bubbleColor] ); await telnyx.messages.create({ from: fromNumber, to: to, text: message }); sseNotify(integratorId, 'new_message', { phone_number: to, role: 'human', message, sender_name: senderName, bubble_color: bubbleColor, created_at: new Date().toISOString() }); res.json({ success: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Send error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Failed to send message' }); } }); // ─── HANDOFF SUMMARY ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────── async function writeHandoffSummary(integratorId, phoneNumber, integratorName) { try { const lastMarkerResult2 = await pool.query( `SELECT MAX(created_at) as last_marker FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 AND role IN ('summary', 'handback')`, [integratorId, phoneNumber] ); const since = lastMarkerResult2.rows[0]?.last_marker || new Date(0); const threadResult = await pool.query( `SELECT role, message, sender_name, bubble_color, created_at FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND phone_number = $2 AND created_at > $3 AND role != 'summary' ORDER BY created_at ASC`, [integratorId, phoneNumber, since] ); const thread = threadResult.rows; const andiMsgs = thread.filter(m => m.role === 'assistant'); if (andiMsgs.length < 2) return; const nameResult = await pool.query( `SELECT cl.first_name, cl.last_name, h.name as household_name FROM client_phones cp LEFT JOIN clients cl ON cl.id = cp.client_id LEFT JOIN households h ON h.id = cl.household_id WHERE cp.integrator_id = $1 AND cp.phone_number = $2 LIMIT 1`, [integratorId, phoneNumber] ); const nameRow = nameResult.rows[0]; const clientName = nameRow?.first_name ? (nameRow.first_name + ' ' + (nameRow.last_name || '')).trim() : (nameRow?.household_name || 'Client'); const andiOnly = thread.filter(m => m.role !== 'human'); const transcript = andiOnly.map(m => `${m.role === 'user' ? clientName : 'Andi'}: ${m.message}` ).join('\n'); const response = await anthropic.messages.create({ model: 'claude-sonnet-4-5', max_tokens: 400, system: `You are summarizing the Andi portion of a client support conversation for ${integratorName}. Return a JSON object with these exact fields: - issue: one sentence describing what the client's issue was - andi_action: one sentence describing what Andi did before the team member jumped in - resolved: false - follow_up_needed: true - follow_up_note: one sentence on what the team member should follow up on, or null if nothing needed - client_mindset: one sentence on the client's tone Return only valid JSON. No preamble. No explanation.`, messages: [{ role: 'user', content: `Client: ${clientName}\n\nTranscript:\n${transcript}` }] }); const raw = response.content[0].text.replace(/\`\`\`json|\`\`\`/g, '').trim(); const parsed = JSON.parse(raw); await pool.query( `INSERT INTO conversations (integrator_id, phone_number, role, message, created_at) VALUES ($1, $2, 'summary', $3, NOW())`, [integratorId, phoneNumber, JSON.stringify(parsed)] ); sseNotify(integratorId, 'new_message', { phone_number: phoneNumber, role: 'summary', message: JSON.stringify(parsed), created_at: new Date().toISOString() }); console.log(`[handoff] Summary written for ${phoneNumber}`); } catch (err) { console.error('[handoff] writeHandoffSummary failed:', err.message); } } // ─── HANDBACK ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.post('/api/handback', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const { phone } = req.body; if (!phone) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'phone required' }); try { await pool.query( `INSERT INTO conversations (integrator_id, phone_number, role, message, created_at) VALUES ($1, $2, 'handback', 'Handed back to Andi', NOW())`, [integratorId, phone] ); sseNotify(integratorId, 'handback', { phone_number: phone }); // v1.56.0: hand back is the other half of the takeover_handback event. Best effort. sendPush(integratorId, 'takeover_handback', { title: 'Handed back to Andi', body: 'Andi is handling the thread again', phone: phone }, { excludeUserId: req.session.userId }).catch(function(){}); res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Handback error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── OPS DASH (v1.35.0, RestWerks operator god view, read-only) ─────────────── // These are the ONLY routes that read across every integrator. Both gates required: // requireAuth (valid session/token) then requireSuperadmin (live is_superadmin flag). app.get('/api/ops/overview', requireAuth, requireSuperadmin, async (req, res) => { try { const r = await pool.query(` SELECT i.id, i.name, i.status, i.telnyx_number, i.is_test, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM integrator_users u WHERE u.integrator_id = i.id AND u.active = true) AS members, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM households h WHERE h.integrator_id = i.id AND h.active = true) AS households, (SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT c.phone_number) FROM conversations c WHERE c.integrator_id = i.id) AS threads, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM conversations c WHERE c.integrator_id = i.id AND c.role = 'user' AND c.created_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '7 days') AS client_msgs_7d, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM conversations c WHERE c.integrator_id = i.id AND c.role = 'assistant' AND c.created_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '7 days') AS andi_msgs_7d, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets t WHERE t.integrator_id = i.id AND t.status = 'open') AS open_tickets, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM follow_ups f WHERE f.integrator_id = i.id AND f.status = 'pending') AS pending_followups, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM andi_flags af WHERE af.integrator_id = i.id AND af.status = 'open') AS open_flags, (SELECT MAX(c.created_at) FROM conversations c WHERE c.integrator_id = i.id) AS last_message_at, (SELECT MAX(u.last_login) FROM integrator_users u WHERE u.integrator_id = i.id) AS last_login_at FROM integrators i ORDER BY last_message_at DESC NULLS LAST, i.name ASC `); res.json({ integrators: r.rows, generated_at: new Date().toISOString() }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ops overview error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error', detail: err.message }); } }); app.get('/api/ops/integrator/:id', requireAuth, requireSuperadmin, async (req, res) => { const id = req.params.id; try { const info = await pool.query( `SELECT id, name, status, telnyx_number, is_test, timezone FROM integrators WHERE id = $1`, [id] ); if (!info.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Integrator not found' }); const members = await pool.query( `SELECT id, first_name, last_name, email, role, active, last_login, created_at FROM integrator_users WHERE integrator_id = $1 ORDER BY (last_login IS NULL), last_login DESC`, [id] ); const messages = await pool.query( `SELECT phone_number, role, message, created_at FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND role IN ('user','assistant','human') ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 50`, [id] ); const tickets = await pool.query( `SELECT id, ticket_type, summary, status, phone_number, opened_at, assigned_to FROM tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND status = 'open' ORDER BY opened_at DESC NULLS LAST LIMIT 25`, [id] ); const flags = await pool.query( `SELECT id, phone_number, note, flagged_by, created_at FROM andi_flags WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND status = 'open' ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 25`, [id] ); res.json({ integrator: info.rows[0], members: members.rows, messages: messages.rows, open_tickets: tickets.rows, open_flags: flags.rows }); } catch (err) { console.error('Ops integrator detail error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error', detail: err.message }); } }); // ─── SSE EVENTS ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── app.get('/api/events', async (req, res) => { const token = req.query.token; let integratorId = null; if (req.session && req.session.integratorId) { integratorId = req.session.integratorId; } else if (token) { try { const row = await getUserFromToken(token); if (row) integratorId = row.integrator_id; } catch (err) {} } if (!integratorId) return res.status(401).end(); res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'text/event-stream', 'Cache-Control': 'no-cache', 'Connection': 'keep-alive', 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': req.headers.origin || '*', 'Access-Control-Allow-Credentials': 'true' }); res.write('event: connected\ndata: {}\n\n'); if (!sseClients[integratorId]) sseClients[integratorId] = []; sseClients[integratorId].push(res); const keepAlive = setInterval(() => { // A real event, not an SSE comment: browsers do not expose comments to JS, so the dashboard // could never tell a silently dead pipe from a quiet one. A ping event lets it watchdog. try { res.write('event: ping\ndata: {}\n\n'); } catch(e) { clearInterval(keepAlive); } }, 25000); req.on('close', () => { clearInterval(keepAlive); sseClients[integratorId] = (sseClients[integratorId] || []).filter(r => r !== res); }); }); app.post('/api/notify', async (req, res) => { const { integrator_id, phone_number, role, message, sender_name, bubble_color, created_at, ticket_push, media } = req.body; if (!integrator_id) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'integrator_id required' }); if (role === 'handback') { // Client texted Andi or the resume sweep pulled the thread back. Flip the // open thread UI the same way the manual handback does. sseNotify(integrator_id, 'handback', { phone_number }); sendPush(integrator_id, 'takeover_handback', { title: 'Handed back to Andi', body: 'Andi is handling the thread again', phone: phone_number }, {}).catch(function(){}); } else { // v1.70.0: THE EVENT CARRIES THE MEDIA. Until now the payload was text only, so a photo // arriving live had nothing in the event to render and the dashboard fell back to inferring // one from the message text. That inference only ever matched the bare [photo] shape, so a // photo WITH a caption was invisible until some later message forced a reload. Normalized // to an array of plain strings and capped, because this is an enrichment riding a route // that must never fail: a bad media field degrades to the old text-only behaviour rather // than throwing inside the notify. // v1.70.1: SHAPE AGNOSTIC. This route does not own the media shape and must not parse it. // The only question any consumer asks of this payload is whether there IS media: the entries // are R2 keys and the browser-usable signed URL is minted by the thread read, so nothing // downstream reads a field off these objects. v1.70.0 reached for url, ref and key, the // webhook sends r2_key, and the result was a non-empty array arriving and an empty one // leaving, silently, which looked identical to the field never being sent. let mediaRefs = []; try { if (Array.isArray(media)) { mediaRefs = media .filter(function (m) { return m !== null && (typeof m === 'string' || typeof m === 'object'); }) .slice(0, 10); } } catch (e) { mediaRefs = []; } sseNotify(integrator_id, 'new_message', { phone_number, role, message, sender_name, bubble_color, created_at, media: mediaRefs }); // v1.56.0: an inbound CLIENT message is the core push event (the Chris build). Resolve the // sender name for the title and whether the number is attached (an unattached number also // reaches users with the unknown_number pref on). Best effort, never blocks the notify ack. if (role === 'user') { (async function() { let title = 'New client message'; let unknown = false; try { const who = await pool.query( `SELECT cl.first_name, cl.last_name, h.name AS household_name FROM client_phones cp LEFT JOIN clients cl ON cl.id = cp.client_id LEFT JOIN households h ON h.id = cl.household_id WHERE cp.integrator_id = $1 AND cp.phone_number = $2 AND cp.active = true LIMIT 1`, [integrator_id, phone_number] ); if (who.rows.length && who.rows[0].first_name) { const w = who.rows[0]; title = (w.first_name + ' ' + (w.last_name || '')).trim() + (w.household_name ? ' (' + w.household_name + ')' : ''); } else { unknown = true; title = 'Unknown number'; } } catch (e) { } sendPush(integrator_id, 'client_message', { title: title, body: message || '', phone: phone_number }, { unknown: unknown }).catch(function(){}); })(); } // v1.57.0: webhook ticket moments (Andi opened a ticket, client reports resolved) ride the // marker notify with a ticket_push block. Relays under the existing new_ticket pref. if (ticket_push && ticket_push.title) { sendPush(integrator_id, 'new_ticket', { title: ticket_push.title, body: ticket_push.body || '', phone: phone_number }, { assigneeId: ticket_push.assignee_id || null }).catch(function(){}); } } res.json({ ok: true }); }); // ─── HOME STATS (v1.61.0) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── // Replaces the hardcoded home panel. Every field here is computed or null; nothing // is estimated, defaulted to a friendly number, or carried over from a mockup. // The rule that matters: NULL when there is nothing to compute. A new integrator // with no traffic sees blanks, not a 0 percent resolution rate that reads like a // failing grade, and not an invented one that reads like a lie. const HOME_WINDOWS = { '24h': '24 hours', '7d': '7 days', '30d': '30 days' }; // v1.67.0: category matching, scored. Replaces the v1.63.0 first-match ladder. // // STILL TEMPORARY, same as before: this exists because the webhook computes a category // and discards it (saveResolution is called with null), and the one line fix that stores // it is gated behind the Sept 8 webhook touch. DELETE THIS once the category is stored on // write and read the column instead. // // WHY SCORED INSTEAD OF A LADDER: a ladder returns whichever rule sits highest, not // whichever fits best, so the answer depended on the order fourteen lines happened to be // written in. Live examples that proved it: // "loft shade that won't go down and a broken great room remote" -> control_system // "primary bedroom remote was completely dead" -> audio // The first is a shade issue that lost to the word remote; the second is a control issue // that lost to audio. Now every category is scored by how many of its terms appear and the // highest score wins, with ladder order surviving only as the tiebreaker. const CATEGORY_TERMS = [ ['battery_alert', ['battery', 'batteries', 'beep', 'beeping', 'chirp', 'chirping', 'low battery']], ['reboot_required', ['reboot', 'restart', 'power cycle', 'unplug', 'hard reset', 'rebooted']], ['smoke_fire_detector', ['smoke', 'fire alarm', 'smoke detector', 'carbon monoxide', 'co detector']], // 'offline' deliberately NOT here: it is a SYMPTOM, not a device, and it made "camera at // the front door is offline" a network issue. A device noun must outrank a symptom word. ['network_internet', ['internet', 'wifi', 'wi-fi', 'network', 'router', 'modem', 'no internet', 'access point']], ['tv_video', ['tv', 'television', 'screen', 'hdmi', 'display', 'picture', 'appletv', 'apple tv', 'roku', 'black screen', 'no picture', 'projector']], ['audio', ['audio', 'sound', 'speaker', 'speakers', 'music', 'volume', 'sonos', 'no sound', 'subwoofer', 'soundbar']], // 'app' and 'elan app' deliberately NOT here: an app is how a client describes ANY issue // ("the lights zone shows in the Elan app but does not turn on"), so it dragged lighting, // shade and audio problems into control. Control means the control DEVICE is the problem. ['control_system', ['control4', 'remote', 'crestron', 'savant', 'automation', 'control system', 'touch panel', 'touchscreen', 'keypad not']], ['lighting', ['light', 'lights', 'lighting', 'dimmer', 'bulb', 'light switch', 'lutron', 'landscape lighting', 'zone']], ['security_system', ['alarm', 'security', 'dsc', 'qolsys', 'iq4', 'arm', 'disarm', 'security panel']], ['camera_doorbell', ['camera', 'cameras', 'doorbell', 'nvr', 'surveillance', 'recording']], ['door_lock', ['door lock', 'schlage', 'yale', 'deadbolt']], // "won't go down" / "wont go down" are here because a shade issue that also mentions a // remote otherwise ties with control and loses on order. The SYMPTOM is the signal that // says which device is actually broken. ['shade_blind', ['shade', 'shades', 'blind', 'blinds', 'shutter', 'motorized', "won't go down", 'wont go down', "won't go up", 'wont go up']], ['thermostat', ['thermostat', 'hvac', 'temperature', 'furnace']], ['ups_power', ['ups', 'wattbox', 'power strip', 'outlet', 'breaker']] ]; // Scores one issue line and returns the best category. Runs in NODE, not SQL, on purpose: // generating this as a SQL expression produced a 219,000 character query, and the volume // here is one row per closed session in the window, which is tens of rows. Scoring here // also makes it directly testable without a database. // // MULTI WORD TERMS COUNT DOUBLE, because a two word phrase is a far stronger signal than a // single common noun: "light switch" should beat a bare "switch" appearing anywhere. // // ANTI TERMS are the second half of the rule and they encode a real domain fact: "the // remote was dead even after replacing the batteries" is NOT a battery issue, it is a // remote issue where the battery has already been ruled out. Without this it scored battery // 1 and control 1, the tie sent it to battery, and two near identical summaries landed in // two different categories, which is how the problem showed itself. const CATEGORY_ANTI = { battery_alert: ['after replacing', 'replaced the batter', 'new batteries', 'even after'] }; function scoreCategory(text) { const t = (text || '').toLowerCase(); if (!t.trim()) return null; let best = null, bestScore = 0; CATEGORY_TERMS.forEach(function (entry, idx) { const name = entry[0], terms = entry[1]; let score = 0; for (const term of terms) if (t.indexOf(term) !== -1) score += (term.indexOf(' ') !== -1 ? 2 : 1); if (!score) return; const anti = CATEGORY_ANTI[name] || []; for (const a of anti) if (t.indexOf(a) !== -1) score -= 2; if (score <= 0) return; // idx only ever breaks a tie; it can never outweigh a real term hit. const adjusted = score - idx / 1000; if (adjusted > bestScore) { bestScore = adjusted; best = name; } }); return best || 'other'; } app.get('/api/home-stats', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; // Whitelist, never interpolate user input into an interval. const key = HOME_WINDOWS[req.query.window] ? req.query.window : '24h'; const iv = HOME_WINDOWS[key]; try { // Threads with client traffic in the window. This is the CONVERSATIONS tile only. // It is deliberately NOT the denominator of the resolution rate: a thread is a phone // number that lives for months across many separate sessions, so sessions over // threads is not a rate, it is sessions per thread. That was the 1.61.0 bug. const activity = await pool.query( `SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT phone_number)::int AS threads FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND role = 'user' AND created_at > NOW() - $2::interval`, [integratorId, iv] ); const threads = activity.rows[0].threads; // THE RESOLUTION RATE, one session at a time. // DENOMINATOR: sessions Andi closed (one summary row each). // NUMERATOR: those that did NOT produce a ticket. // Numerator and denominator are the same unit, which is what makes it a rate. // A session is escalated when a ticket was opened on that phone between the session // start and ten minutes past its close; the grace covers a ticket written at close. // A session with no earlier summary on that thread starts 24 hours back rather than // at the beginning of time, the same bound the message count uses below. const rateSql = ` WITH sums AS ( SELECT phone_number, created_at, GREATEST( COALESCE(LAG(created_at) OVER (PARTITION BY phone_number ORDER BY created_at), created_at - INTERVAL '24 hours'), created_at - INTERVAL '24 hours' ) AS started_at FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND role = 'summary' AND created_at > NOW() - $2::interval ) SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS sessions, COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE NOT EXISTS ( SELECT 1 FROM tickets t WHERE t.integrator_id = $1 AND t.phone_number = s.phone_number AND t.opened_at > s.started_at AND t.opened_at <= s.created_at + INTERVAL '10 minutes' ))::int AS clean FROM sums s`; const rateRow = await pool.query(rateSql, [integratorId, iv]); const resolved = rateRow.rows[0].sessions; const rate = resolved > 0 ? Math.round((rateRow.rows[0].clean / resolved) * 100) : null; // The trend rows run the IDENTICAL computation over three windows, so the three // numbers under the headline are comparable to it and to each other. They can fall, // which the 1.61.0 versions structurally could not. const [t7, t30, tall] = await Promise.all([ pool.query(rateSql, [integratorId, '7 days']), pool.query(rateSql, [integratorId, '30 days']), // All time strips the window clause, so it takes ONE parameter, not two. Passing // the extra one would fail the bind ("supplies 2 parameters, requires 1"). pool.query(rateSql.replace("AND created_at > NOW() - $2::interval", ""), [integratorId]) ]); const pctOf = (r) => (r.rows[0].sessions > 0 ? Math.round((r.rows[0].clean / r.rows[0].sessions) * 100) : null); // v1.64.0: AFTER HOURS. The one number that is the product. Evaluated in the // integrator's own timezone against a 7am to 5pm weekday business day, so a Saturday // afternoon counts as after hours, which is what it is for a client with a dead TV. const tzRow = await pool.query('SELECT COALESCE(timezone, $2) AS tz FROM integrators WHERE id = $1', [integratorId, 'America/Chicago']); const tz = tzRow.rows[0] ? tzRow.rows[0].tz : 'America/Chicago'; const afterHours = await pool.query( `WITH local AS ( SELECT (created_at AT TIME ZONE 'UTC') AT TIME ZONE $3 AS lt FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND role = 'summary' AND created_at > NOW() - $2::interval ) SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS total, -- Outside 7am to 5pm weekdays. The headline. COUNT(*) FILTER ( WHERE EXTRACT(DOW FROM lt) IN (0,6) OR EXTRACT(HOUR FROM lt) < 7 OR EXTRACT(HOUR FROM lt) >= 17 )::int AS after_hours, -- 9pm to 6am. The ones that would otherwise have been a morning callback, -- and the number that actually lands when an owner reads it. COUNT(*) FILTER ( WHERE EXTRACT(HOUR FROM lt) >= 21 OR EXTRACT(HOUR FROM lt) < 6 )::int AS overnight, -- Saturday and Sunday. Overlaps overnight on purpose: a 2am Sunday is both, -- and forcing these into exclusive buckets would make each one misleading. COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE EXTRACT(DOW FROM lt) IN (0,6))::int AS weekend FROM local`, [integratorId, iv, tz] ); const ahRow = afterHours.rows[0]; const ahTotal = ahRow.total; const ahCount = ahRow.after_hours; // v1.64.0: TICKET RESPONSE TIME, opened to the first human reply. Real tonight. // Resolution time is intentionally NOT computed here: see the header. The column // exists, it stays null until the status model gives it an honest number. const ticketClock = await pool.query( `SELECT ROUND(AVG(first_response_seconds)::numeric / 60, 1) AS avg_minutes, COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND first_response_seconds IS NOT NULL AND opened_at > NOW() - $2::interval`, [integratorId, iv] ); // Ticket categories for THIS integrator. These are escalations, which is a // narrower and more honest claim than the old card made. const cats = await pool.query( `SELECT issue_category AS category, COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND issue_category IS NOT NULL AND issue_category <> '' GROUP BY issue_category ORDER BY n DESC LIMIT 5`, [integratorId] ); const catTotal = cats.rows.reduce((a, c) => a + c.n, 0); const categories = cats.rows.map(c => ({ category: c.category, count: c.n, pct: catTotal > 0 ? Math.round((c.n / catTotal) * 100) : 0 })); // VOLUME. messages_handled counts every real turn in the window, client and Andi // both, which is the number that reads as work absorbed rather than as a ratio. const volume = await pool.query( `SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS messages FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND role IN ('user','assistant') AND created_at > NOW() - $2::interval`, [integratorId, iv] ); // ISSUES BY CATEGORY, read from Andi's own summary. // The summary row's message is JSON written by analyzeConversation, and its `issue` // field is Andi's one line statement of what the conversation was about. That is the // right input: it is written by the model that handled the conversation, with the // small talk already stripped. Two earlier attempts read raw client text instead and // produced mostly Other, because a summary is written by the 07:00 cron and its // window sweeps up a whole day of unrelated talk. See the header for the detail. // // WHY THE GUARD: these rows are TEXT and nothing has ever validated them as JSON, so // one malformed row would fail the whole query on a hard cast and take the panel down. // The check is left(ltrim(message),1) = '{' rather than a regex ON PURPOSE: this string // passes through a Python build script, a JS template literal, and then Postgres, and a // backslash means something different in all three. A regex here would be three layers // of escaping guarding one character. This is the same test with nothing to get wrong. const issues = await pool.query( `WITH s AS ( SELECT CASE WHEN left(ltrim(message), 1) = '{' THEN NULLIF(trim(COALESCE(message::json->>'issue', '')), '') ELSE NULL END AS m FROM conversations WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND role = 'summary' AND created_at > NOW() - $2::interval ) SELECT m FROM s`, [integratorId, iv] ); // Grouped in Node so the scoring above stays testable without a database. Same shape out. const issueCounts = {}; for (const row of issues.rows) { const cat = (row.m == null || !String(row.m).trim()) ? 'uncategorized' : scoreCategory(row.m); issueCounts[cat] = (issueCounts[cat] || 0) + 1; } const issueTotal = issues.rows.length; const andi_issues = Object.keys(issueCounts) .map(k => ({ category: k, count: issueCounts[k], pct: issueTotal > 0 ? Math.round((issueCounts[k] / issueTotal) * 100) : 0 })) .sort((a, b) => b.count - a.count) .slice(0, 8); const houses = await pool.query( `SELECT (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM households WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND active = true)::int AS total, (SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT h.id) FROM households h JOIN client_phones cp ON cp.household_id = h.id JOIN conversations c ON c.phone_number = cp.phone_number AND c.integrator_id = h.integrator_id WHERE h.integrator_id = $1 AND h.active = true AND c.role = 'user' AND c.created_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '7 days')::int AS active_7d`, [integratorId] ); // v1.68.0: the resolution clock, which shipped as a blank row with a note in 1.64.0. // ONLY tickets that were actually clocked count. A ticket carrying a 'backfill' event was // resolved before the clock existed and holds closed_at minus opened_at, which is precisely // the number John is complaining about; averaging those in would report his own complaint // back to him as the fix. So the average starts empty and fills as real work resolves, and // the sample size rides along so the dashboard can stay blank until it means something. // CLOSED tickets are excluded entirely rather than counted as zero: a duplicate that ended in // four seconds is not a fast resolution, it is not a resolution. // // THE PREDICATE IS THE CLOSING BACKFILL, NOT ANY BACKFILL, and the first version of this got // it wrong. The opening backfill stamps every existing ticket, live ones included, so testing // for any backfill row dropped a ticket that was merely OPENED before the deploy even though // its clock ran for real afterward. Matching on the closing row (entered_by 'backfill' AND // the ticket's own terminal status, the same guard the backfill itself used) means only a // ticket that ENDED before the clock existed is excluded. A ticket that straddles the deploy // is counted and reads short, because short is what was actually measured; ticket_resolution_n // rides along so the dashboard can hold the number back until the sample is worth showing. const resClock = await pool.query( `SELECT ROUND(AVG(t.resolution_seconds)::numeric / 60, 1) AS avg_minutes, COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.integrator_id = $1 AND ${KIND_SQL} = 'resolved' AND t.resolution_seconds IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM ticket_status_events e WHERE e.ticket_id = t.id AND e.entered_by = 'backfill' AND e.status = t.status)`, [integratorId] ); const work = await pool.query( `SELECT (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.integrator_id = $1 AND ${KIND_SQL} = 'open')::int AS open_tickets, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.integrator_id = $1 AND ${KIND_SQL} = 'scheduled')::int AS scheduled_tickets, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets t ${KIND_JOIN} WHERE t.integrator_id = $1 AND ${KIND_SQL} = 'waiting')::int AS waiting_tickets, (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM follow_ups WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND status = 'pending')::int AS follow_ups`, [integratorId] ); res.json({ window: key, conversations: threads, conversations_handled: resolved, messages_handled: volume.rows[0].messages, andi_issues, resolved_by_andi: resolved, resolution_rate: rate, resolution_trend: { d7: pctOf(t7), d30: pctOf(t30), all: pctOf(tall) }, after_hours_conversations: ahCount, after_hours_pct: ahTotal > 0 ? Math.round((ahCount / ahTotal) * 100) : null, overnight_conversations: ahRow.overnight, weekend_conversations: ahRow.weekend, ticket_response_minutes: ticketClock.rows[0].n > 0 ? Number(ticketClock.rows[0].avg_minutes) : null, ticket_resolution_minutes: resClock.rows[0].n > 0 ? Number(resClock.rows[0].avg_minutes) : null, ticket_resolution_n: resClock.rows[0].n, categories, households: houses.rows[0].total, households_active_7d: houses.rows[0].active_7d, open_tickets: work.rows[0].open_tickets, scheduled_tickets: work.rows[0].scheduled_tickets, waiting_tickets: work.rows[0].waiting_tickets, follow_ups: work.rows[0].follow_ups }); } catch (err) { console.error('Home stats error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── IMPORTS + ARCHIVE (v1.60.0) ────────────────────────────────────────────── // Storage and reads only this slice. NOTHING here parses a file or writes a person: the Freshdesk // parse, the contact to household matching, and the resolution extraction are their own build. // The point of landing storage first is that the import then runs SERVER SIDE from stored bytes // and can be re run against a better matcher without asking anyone to upload 949 tickets again. // Upload an export file. Owner only, because this is company scale data arriving. app.post('/api/imports/upload', requireAuth, requireOwner, uploadImport.single('file'), async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; if (!req.file) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'file required' }); const source = (req.body.source || 'freshdesk').trim().toLowerCase(); const kind = (req.body.kind || '').trim().toLowerCase() || null; const notes = (req.body.notes || '').trim() || null; try { const ext = (req.file.originalname.split('.').pop() || 'bin').toLowerCase(); const key = `imports/${integratorId}/${source}/${Date.now()}.${ext}`; await r2PutObject(key, req.file.buffer, req.file.mimetype); const ins = await pool.query( `INSERT INTO import_batches (integrator_id, source, kind, r2_key, filename, mime_type, size_bytes, status, notes, created_by) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,$7,'uploaded',$8,$9) RETURNING id, created_at`, [integratorId, source, kind, key, req.file.originalname, req.file.mimetype, req.file.size, notes, await actorName(req)] ); res.json({ ok: true, batch_id: ins.rows[0].id, key, filename: req.file.originalname, size_bytes: req.file.size }); } catch (err) { console.error('Import upload error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Every batch this integrator has uploaded, newest first. app.get('/api/imports', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; try { const r = await pool.query( `SELECT id, source, kind, filename, mime_type, size_bytes, row_count, matched_count, review_count, error_count, status, notes, stats, created_by, created_at, completed_at FROM import_batches WHERE integrator_id = $1 ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 100`, [integratorId] ); res.json(r.rows); } catch (err) { console.error('Imports list error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // One batch, with live counts of what it produced rather than only what it recorded about itself. app.get('/api/imports/:id', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad id' }); try { const b = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM import_batches WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!b.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Batch not found' }); const row = b.rows[0]; const c = await pool.query( `SELECT review_status, COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM import_contacts WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND batch_id = $2 GROUP BY review_status`, [integratorId, id] ); const t = await pool.query( `SELECT review_status, COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM archive_tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND batch_id = $2 GROUP BY review_status`, [integratorId, id] ); row.contact_counts = c.rows; row.ticket_counts = t.rows; res.json(row); } catch (err) { console.error('Import read error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // A signed URL to the stored file, so the import can be re run from the original bytes. app.get('/api/imports/:id/file', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad id' }); try { const b = await pool.query('SELECT r2_key, filename FROM import_batches WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!b.rows.length || !b.rows[0].r2_key) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'File not found' }); const cmd = new GetObjectCommand({ Bucket: process.env.R2_BUCKET_NAME, Key: b.rows[0].r2_key }); const url = await getSignedUrl(r2, cmd, { expiresIn: 3600 }); res.json({ url, filename: b.rows[0].filename }); } catch (err) { console.error('Import file error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Drop a batch and its staging rows. REFUSES once archive rows point at it: a batch is the unit of // undo for a bad run, never a way to delete four years of history with one click. Clear the archive // rows first, deliberately, if that is really what is wanted. app.delete('/api/imports/:id', requireAuth, requireOwner, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad id' }); try { const b = await pool.query('SELECT r2_key FROM import_batches WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!b.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Batch not found' }); const held = await pool.query('SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM archive_tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND batch_id = $2', [integratorId, id]); if (held.rows[0].n > 0) { return res.status(409).json({ error: `This batch produced ${held.rows[0].n} archived tickets. Remove those first if you really want it gone.` }); } await pool.query('DELETE FROM import_batches WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (b.rows[0].r2_key) { try { await r2.send(new DeleteObjectCommand({ Bucket: process.env.R2_BUCKET_NAME, Key: b.rows[0].r2_key })); } catch (e) {} } res.json({ ok: true }); } catch (err) { console.error('Import delete error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // Archive list. Always paginated and hard capped: this table holds every ticket an integrator ever // wrote, so an unbounded read is a promise to get slow later. app.get('/api/archive/tickets', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const householdId = req.query.household_id ? parseInt(req.query.household_id, 10) : null; const review = (req.query.review_status || '').trim() || null; const q = (req.query.q || '').trim(); const limit = Math.max(1, Math.min(100, parseInt(req.query.limit, 10) || 50)); const offset = Math.max(0, parseInt(req.query.offset, 10) || 0); const vals = [integratorId]; let where = 'integrator_id = $1'; if (householdId) { vals.push(householdId); where += ` AND household_id = $${vals.length}`; } if (review) { vals.push(review); where += ` AND review_status = $${vals.length}`; } if (q) { vals.push(`%${q}%`); where += ` AND (subject ILIKE $${vals.length} OR requester_name ILIKE $${vals.length} OR requester_email ILIKE $${vals.length})`; } try { const total = await pool.query(`SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS n FROM archive_tickets WHERE ${where}`, vals); vals.push(limit); vals.push(offset); const r = await pool.query( `SELECT id, source, source_ticket_id, subject, status_label, priority_label, ticket_type, requester_name, requester_email, opened_at, resolved_at, closed_at, household_id, client_id, review_status, match_confidence, note_count, resolution_text IS NOT NULL AS has_resolution FROM archive_tickets WHERE ${where} ORDER BY opened_at DESC NULLS LAST, id DESC LIMIT $${vals.length - 1} OFFSET $${vals.length}`, vals ); res.json({ total: total.rows[0].n, limit, offset, tickets: r.rows }); } catch (err) { console.error('Archive list error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // One archived ticket with its full reply thread, oldest first. app.get('/api/archive/tickets/:id', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad id' }); try { const t = await pool.query('SELECT * FROM archive_tickets WHERE id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2', [id, integratorId]); if (!t.rows.length) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Ticket not found' }); const n = await pool.query( `SELECT id, source_note_id, seq, body, from_name, from_email, is_client, is_private, noise, created_at_src FROM archive_notes WHERE archive_ticket_id = $1 AND integrator_id = $2 ORDER BY seq ASC, created_at_src ASC NULLS LAST, id ASC`, [id, integratorId] ); const row = t.rows[0]; row.notes = n.rows; res.json(row); } catch (err) { console.error('Archive read error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // What a household's history looks like at a glance, for the household surface later. app.get('/api/households/:id/archive', requireAuth, async (req, res) => { const integratorId = req.session.integratorId; const id = parseInt(req.params.id, 10); if (!Number.isInteger(id)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Bad id' }); try { const c = await pool.query( `SELECT COUNT(*)::int AS n, MIN(opened_at) AS first_at, MAX(opened_at) AS last_at FROM archive_tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND household_id = $2`, [integratorId, id] ); const r = await pool.query( `SELECT id, source_ticket_id, subject, status_label, opened_at, closed_at, note_count FROM archive_tickets WHERE integrator_id = $1 AND household_id = $2 ORDER BY opened_at DESC NULLS LAST, id DESC LIMIT 25`, [integratorId, id] ); res.json({ count: c.rows[0].n, first_at: c.rows[0].first_at, last_at: c.rows[0].last_at, recent: r.rows }); } catch (err) { console.error('Household archive error:', err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'Server error' }); } }); // ─── START ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── initDb().then(() => { const PORT = process.env.PORT || 3001; app.listen(PORT, () => console.log(`RestWerks portal v1.71.0 running on port ${PORT}`)); });
